\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}
is composed of the rest of the
(r_{k}-r_{k}^{*})
columns of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}
. Then the projection operator onto
{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}
, the orthogonal complement of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}
, satisfies

P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}}={\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}}={\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}}-P_{\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}}=({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}}).

(17)

This implies
\|({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}}){\mathbf{Z}}\|\leq\|({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}}){\mathbf{Z}}\|
for any matrix
{\mathbf{Z}}
with compatible dimension. Based on this property, we can focus on the first
r_{k}^{*}
columns of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}
and establish the following lemma, which plays a key role in establishing the convergence of RGD and RGN.

Lemma 3
(An Over-parameterized Projection Error Bound)

Suppose
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
is an order-
(d+m)
Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}:=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d+m})
tensor and
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
is an order-
(d+m)
Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}^{*}:=(r^{*}_{1},\ldots,r^{*}_{d+m})
tensor with
\mathbf{r}^{*}\leq\mathbf{r}
. Then we have

\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\frac{2(d+m)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}}{\underline{\lambda}},

where
P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}})_{\perp}}:={\mathbf{I}}-P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}
is the orthogonal complement of the projector
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}
(
5
) and
\underline{\lambda}:=\min_{k=1,\ldots,d+m}\sigma_{r^{*}_{k}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}))
. Especially in the matrix setting, i.e.,
d+m=2
, a sharper upper bound holds:
\|P_{(T_{{\mathbf{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{2\|{\mathbf{X}}^{t}-{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{\sigma_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})}.

Initialization Guarantees for Scalar-on-tensor Regression and Tensor-on-vector Regression
. A key step of Algorithms
2
and
3
is the one-iteration HOOI (OHOOI) algorithm (Algorithm
5
below). Such one loop update improves the dependence of
r^{*}
in sample complexity compared to the vanilla T-HOSVD based initialization in both scalar-on-tensor and tensor-on-vector regressions. In the proofs of Theorems
5
and
6
, we develop the following deterministic tensor perturbation bound for OHOOI in the over-parameterized regime.

Algorithm 5

One-iteration Higher-Order Orthogonal Iteration (OHOOI)

1:

Input:

\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
, initialization
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}},k=1,\ldots,d
, input Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d})
.

2:

For
k=1
to
d
, update
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{1}={\rm{SVD}}_{r_{k}}\big(\mathcal{M}_{k}(\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}\times_{j<k}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top}\times_{j>k}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top})\big).

3:

Output:

\widehat{{\mathbfcal{T}}}=\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}\times_{k=1}^{d}P_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{1}}
.

Theorem 10
(Perturbation Bound for Over-parameterized Tensor Decomposition)

Suppose
\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}},{\mathbfcal{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
,
{\mathbfcal{T}}
is of Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}^{*}=(r^{*}_{1},\ldots,r^{*}_{d})
with Tucker decomposition
{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times\cdots\times_{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{d}
, where
{\mathbfcal{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}^{*}\times\cdots\times r_{d}^{*}}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}^{*}}
for
k=1,\ldots,d
. Let
{\mathbfcal{Z}}=\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}-{\mathbfcal{T}}
. Suppose the inputs of the OHOOI algorithm are
\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}
, Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d})
with
\mathbf{r}\geq\mathbf{r}^{*}
and initializations
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
for
k=1,\ldots,d
. If the initialization error satisfies
\max_{k=1,\ldots,d}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{0\top}_{k\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}
. Then the output of Algorithm
5
,
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{T}}}
, satisfies
\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{T}}}-{\mathbfcal{T}}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq(2^{\frac{d+1}{2}}\cdot d+1)\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
.

Low-degree Polynomials Evidence for Problems With Correlated Structures.
As we have mentioned in Remark
7
, the main task in the proof of Theorem
9
is to compute the norm of the truncated likelihood ratio. See Supplement
G.2
for a preliminary of low-degree polynomials method. Since the data are i.i.d. Gaussian under the null hypothesis of (
14
), the main challenge boils down to computing the expected Hermite polynomials on correlated multivariate Gaussian. In the following Lemma
4
, we provide a simple formula for that. This lemma can be useful in establishing low-degree polynomial hardness evidence for other problems with complex structures. Let
\{h_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be the normalized univariate Hermite polynomials
h_{k}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{k!}}H_{k}
where
\{H_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are univariate Hermite polynomials which are defined by the following recurrence:
H_{0}(x)=1,H_{1}(x)=x
,
H_{k+1}(x)=xH_{k}(x)-kH_{k-1}(x)
for
k\geq 1
.

Lemma 4
(Expected Hermitian Polynomials on Correlated Multivariate Gaussian)

Suppose
w
is a positive integer,
Y\in\mathbb{R},{\mathbf{X}}=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{w})\in\mathbb{R}^{w}
are random variable and random vectors, respectively, and
(Y,{\mathbf{X}})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}1&{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{u}}&{\mathbf{I}}_{w}\end{bmatrix}\right)
with
{\mathbf{u}}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{w})
. For any integers
\alpha,\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{w}\geq 0
,
\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}(Y)\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(X_{j})\right)=\sqrt{\frac{\alpha!}{\prod_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}!}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{\beta_{j}}1(\alpha=\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j})
, where
1(\cdot)
in the indicator function.

7
Implementation Details of RGD and RGN

In this section, we complement the implementation details of RGD and RGN proposed in Section
2.2
.

Implementation of RGD.
First, by the definition of the adjoint map,
\mathscr{A}^{*}:\mathbb{R}^{n\times p_{d+1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}\to\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
satisfies
\langle\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}),{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{2}\rangle=\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1},\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Z}}_{2})\rangle
for any
{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p_{d+1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
. Simple manipulation yields:

\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Z}}_{2})_{[k_{1},\ldots,k_{d},j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{2[i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i[k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}]}.

Combining this with the formula of projection
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}
in (
5
), we can calculate
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-\alpha_{t}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})-{\mathbfcal{Y}})
and implement the RGD update.

Implementation of RGN.
To illustrate the implementation details of RGN, we first introduce the following lemma.

Lemma 5
(Spectrum of
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}
)

Suppose
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}
is of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}
and the linear map
\mathscr{A}
satisfies the 2
\mathbf{r}
-TRIP. Then for any tensor
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
,

(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}\leq(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}},

(18)

and

\frac{\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}}{1+R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\leq\|(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})^{-1}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\frac{\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}.

(19)

Lemma
5
shows the linear operator
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}
, which is a mapping from
T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
to itself, is provably invertible under TRIP condition, which further implies the least squares in RGN update,
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}=\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{X}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}
, has a unique solution. In the following Proposition
2
, we show that the RGN update can be reduced to solving
(m+1)
least squares, which renders a fast implementation of RGN.

Proposition 2
(Efficient Implementation of RGN Update)

Suppose
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}
has Tucker decomposition
{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}
. Then the RGN update, i.e.,
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}=\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{X}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}
, is equal to
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}={\mathbfcal{B}}^{t}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}
, where

•

({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\}_{k=1}^{d})
is the solution of the following least squares with design matrix size
n\prod_{l=d+1}^{d+m}r_{l}\times(\prod_{k=1}^{d+m}r_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{d}r_{k}(p_{k}-r_{k}))
:

\begin{split}&({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\}_{k=1}^{d})\\
=&\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbfcal{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},\\
{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{k}-r_{k})\times r_{k}},k=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{B}}\rangle_{*}\\
&-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\rangle_{*}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
=&\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbfcal{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},\\
{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{k}-r_{k})\times r_{k}},k=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{l}\in[r_{d+l}],l=1,\ldots,m}\\
&~~\Big(\left({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}\right)_{[j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{B}}_{[:,\ldots,:,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}\rangle-\\
&-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}\left({\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top}\right)\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}_{[:,\ldots,:,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]})\right)^{\top},{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\rangle\Big)^{2}\end{split}

•

for
k=d+1,\ldots,d+m
,

{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t\top}=\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times(p_{k}-r_{k})}}\|{\mathbf{Y}}_{ki}-{\mathbf{A}}_{ki}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}^{2},

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{ki}=\left(\mathcal{M}_{k-d}(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\rangle_{*})\right)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{\prod_{l=d+1,l\neq k}^{d+m}r_{l}\times r_{k}}
,
{\mathbf{Y}}_{ki}=\left(\mathcal{M}_{k-d}({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l\neq k-d}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top})\right)^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{\prod_{l=d+1,l\neq k}^{d+m}r_{l}\times(p_{k}-r_{k})}
.

In the tensor-on-vector regression (
d=1
), the update of RGN has a cleaner and fully closed expression as follows.

Lemma 6
(RGN Update in Tensor-on-vector Regression)

Consider the RGN for tensor-on-vector regression in (
11
). Suppose
{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}
is invertible where
{\mathbf{A}}
is the collection of covariate vectors and the iterate at iteration
t
is
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}=\llbracket{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t};{\mathbf{U}}^{t}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}^{t}_{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{U}}^{t}_{m+1}\rrbracket
. Then the solution
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}
in (
8
) has a closed-form expression:

{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}={\mathbfcal{B}}^{t}\times_{k=1}^{1+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{1+m}{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t},

where

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t})=({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t})^{-1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}})\otimes_{j=(1+m)}^{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}-{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}({\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}){\mathbf{W}}^{t}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}^{t\top}_{1}\right);\\
&\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{1}\times_{j\neq 1}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t})={\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}({\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}){\mathbf{W}}^{t}_{1}{\mathbf{W}}^{t\top}_{1};\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t})\\
=&{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}^{t}_{k}\left({\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t\top}\left(\otimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq k,\\
i\neq 1\end{subarray}}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{i}}\otimes({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t})\right){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}\right)^{-1}{\mathbf{W}}^{t\top}_{k}\end{split}

for
k=2,\ldots,(1+m)
. Recall
{\mathbf{V}}^{t}_{k}={\rm QR}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t})^{\top})
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}
is defined in (
4
).

8
Numerical Studies

We conduct simulation studies to investigate the numerical performance of RGD/RGN in tensor-on-tensor regression and to verify our theoretical findings. In each simulation, we generate
{\mathbfcal{E}}_{i}
with i.i.d.
N(0,\sigma^{2})
entries,
{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}
with i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries,
\{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{d+m}
uniformly at random from
\mathbb{O}_{p,r^{*}}
for some to-be-specified
p
and
r^{*}
, and
{\mathbfcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{*}\times\cdots\times r^{*}}
with i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries; then we form
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}={\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times\cdots\times_{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{d+m}
and generate
{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,n
. The input rank of RGD and RGN is set to be
\mathbf{r}=(r,\ldots,r)
and
r\geq r^{*}
. In the simulation study, we will experiment with various values of
r
. Additionally,
r
can be chosen by a data-driven approach. See Supplement
C
for details. For simplicity, we mainly focus on two examples: scalar-on-tensor regression and tensor-on-vector regression. In the scalar-on-tensor regression, we consider
d=3
; in the tensor-on-vector regression, we consider
m=3
. Spectral initializations discussed in Section
4
are applied in both examples.

Throughout the simulation studies, the error metric we consider is the relative root mean squared error (Relative RMSE)
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}/\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}
. The algorithm is terminated when it reaches the maximum number of iterations
t_{\max}=300
or the corresponding error metric is less than
10^{-13}
. Unless otherwise noted, the reported results are based on averages of 100 simulations and on a computer with Intel Xeon E5-2680 2.5GHz CPU.

8.1
Numerical Performance of RGD and RGN

In this simulation, we examine the convergence rate of RGD/RGN in over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression and tensor-on-vector regression. We set
\sigma\in\{0,10^{-6},10^{-2}\}
,
p=30
,
r^{*}=3
, and
r=10
. In scalar-on-tensor regression, we choose
n
such that
\frac{n}{p^{3/2}r^{*}}\in\{8,10\}
; in tensor-on-vector regression, we let
\frac{n\underline{\lambda}^{2}}{p^{2}}\in\{2,4\}
where
\underline{\lambda}=\min_{k}\sigma_{r^{*}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}))
. The convergence performance of RGD and RGN in scalar-on-tensor regression and tensor-on-vector regression are presented in Figures
3
and
4
, respectively. In both examples, we find the estimation error of RGD converges linearly to the minimum precision in the noiseless setting and converges linearly to a limit determined by the noise level in the noisy setting. In scalar-on-tensor regression, we find RGN converges quadratically and in tensor-on-vector regression, we observe RGN converges with almost one iteration. We tried several other simulation settings and observed the similar phenomenon.

(a)
Scalar-on-tensor regression: RGD

(b)
Scalar-on-tensor regression: RGN

Fig 3:
Convergence performance of RGD/RGN in over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression with spectral initialization. Here,
p=30,r^{*}=3,r=10
.

(a)
Tensor-on-vector regression: RGD

(b)
Tensor-on-vector regression: RGN

Fig 4:
Convergence performance of RGD/RGN in over-parameterized tensor-on-vector regression with spectral initialization. Here
p=30,r^{*}=3,r=10
.

8.2
Effect of Input Rank and Sample Size on the Performance of RGD and RGN

We also examine the effect of input rank
r
and sample size
n
on the convergence of RGD and RGN and we focus on the scalar-on-tensor regression example. We let
p=30,r^{*}=3
,
\sigma=10^{-6}
,
n\in[500,8000]
and input rank
r\in\{3,6,9,12,15\}
. The performance of RGD and RGN in this simulation study is given in Figure
5
. We can see that for both RGD and RGN, the sample size requirement for convergence increases as the input rank
r
increases. For a fixed
n
, the relative RMSE attainable by RGD and RGN increases as the input rank increases. In addition, the phase transition on the sample complexity for the failure/success in RGN is sharper than the one in RGD. This is because RGN enjoys a higher-order convergence compared to RGD and RGD converges slowly when the number of samples is around the threshold. This matches our main theoretical results in Sections
3
and
4
. Moreover, our results suggest that the number of samples needed for the convergence of RGD and RGN increases at the scale of
r^{d}
for large
r
(here
d=3
) and this is indeed suggested in Figure
6
after we plot the cubic root of the sample size with respect to Relative RMSE.

(a)
RGD

(b)
RGN

Fig 5:
Convergence performance of RGD/RGN in over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression with spectral initialization. Here
p=30,r^{*}=3,n\in[500,8000],r\in\{3,6,9,12,15\}
.

(a)
RGD

(b)
RGN

Fig 6:
Rescaled plot for the convergence performance of RGD/RGN in over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression with spectral initialization. Here
p=30,r^{*}=3,n\in[500,8000],r\in\{3,6,9,12,15\}
.

8.3
Scalar-on-tensor Regression versus Matrix Trace Regression under Over-parameterization

In this simulation, we compare the sample size requirements to ensure successful recovery in over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression and matrix trace regression with an increasing input rank via RGD. We focus on the noiseless setting, i.e.,
\sigma=0
. We say an algorithm achieves successful recovery if the averaged relative root mean squared error (Relative RMSE)
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}/\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}
is smaller than
0.01
. In scalar-on-tensor regression, we set
p=90,r^{*}=1
,
r\in[1,\ldots,8]
,
n=[800,900,\ldots,3500]
and in the matrix trace regression, we set
p=100,r^{*}=1
,
r\in[1,\ldots,8]
and
n=[200,\ldots,3000]
. For every input rank
r
, we increase the sample size by
100
at each time from the one that ensures the successful recovery with input rank
r-1
until RGD succeeds.

Figure
7
shows as the input rank increases, the line of triangles for the sample size requirement of successful recovery in scalar-on-tensor regression is flat at the beginning stage while increases for large input
r
. In contrast, the sample size requirement for successful recovery of RGD in the matrix trace regression always increases linearly as input rank increases. This matches our theoretical findings in Section
5
that a “free lunch” on the sample complexity appears in over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression, but not in the matrix trace regression. Meanwhile, Figure
7
shows when the input rank is equal to
r^{*}
, the phase transitions on sample complexity for the failure/success of RGD in matrix trace regression and scalar-on-tensor regression appear around
n=300\approx 2pr^{*}
and
n=1000\approx p^{3/2}r^{*}
, respectively. This matches the results in Section
5
that there is a statistical-computational gap in scalar-on-tensor regression and
\Omega(p^{d/2})
(here
d=3
) samples are needed for any polynomial-time algorithm to succeed.

Fig 7:
Comparison of successful recovery of RGD under over-parameterized matrix trace regression (Panel (a)) and scalar-on-tensor regression (Panel (b)).

8.4
Comparison of Riemannian Optimization Methods with Existing Algorithms

In the second simulation, we compare RGN with other existing algorithms, including alternating minimization (Alter Mini)
(
Zhou et al., 2013
)
, projected gradient descent (PGD)
(
Rauhut et al., 2017
)
, gradient descent (GD)
(
Han et al., 2022b
)
and scaled gradient descent
(
Tong et al., 2022
)
, in both exact and over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression. While implementing PGD, GD, and scaled GD, we evaluate five choices of step size,
\frac{1}{n}\cdot\{0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1\}
, then choose the best one following
Zheng and Lafferty, 2015
. We set
p=30,r^{*}=3,r\in\{3,10\},n=8p^{3/2}r^{*}
and consider the noiseless case (
\sigma=0
). Figure
8
shows RGN converges quadratically in both settings, while the other baseline algorithms converge at a much slower linear rate. Moreover, when we go from exact-parameterization (Panel (a)) to over-parameterization (Panel (b)), the convergence rate of all baseline algorithms slows down significantly while RGN maintains its robust and fast second-order convergence performance.

Fig 8:
Panel (a):
r=3
; Panel (b):
r=10
. Relative RMSE of RGN (this work), alternating minimization (Alter Mini), projected gradient descent (PGD), gradient descent (GD), and scaled gradient descent (ScaledGD) in noiseless scalar-on-tensor regression.

9
Conclusion and Discussions

In this work, we propose Riemannian gradient descent and Riemannian Gauss-Newton methods for solving the general tensor-on-tensor regression. We provide optimal statistical and computational guarantees for these algorithms in both rank correctly-specified and overspecified settings and discover an intriguing blessing of the statistical-computational gap in the over-parameterized scalar-on-tensor regression. Our current initialization and computational results are established for several representative examples. It is of great interest to see whether these results can be extended to the general tensor-on-tensor regression problem. Moreover, the rank overspecification studied in this paper falls in the moderate over-parameterized regime in the sense that the model still includes more samples than the degree of freedom of parameters. It is interesting to consider the highly over-parameterized regime and study the analogy of implicit regularization effect
(
Gunasekar et al., 2017
;
Li et al., 2018
)
in factorization formulated tensor problems. Some progress has been made recently in the tensor decomposition setting
(
Razin et al., 2021
;
Ge et al., 2021
)
.

Yuetian Luo  and Anru R. Zhang

A
T-HOSVD and ST-HOSVD

In this section, we present the procedures of truncated HOSVD (T-HOSVD)
(
De Lathauwer et al., 2000a
)
and sequentially truncated HOSVD (ST-HOSVD)
(
Vannieuwenhoven et al., 2012
)
. For simplicity, we present the sequentially truncated HOSVD with the truncation order from mode
1
to mode
(d+m)
.

Algorithm 6

Truncated High-order Singular Value Decomposition (T-HOSVD)

1:

Input
:
{\mathbfcal{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
, Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d+m})
.

2:

Compute
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{0}={\rm{SVD}}_{r_{k}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))
for
k=1,\ldots,d+m
.

3:

Output
:
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{Y}}}={\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{0}}
.

Algorithm 7

Sequentially Truncated High-order Singular Value Decomposition (ST-HOSVD)

1:

Input
:
{\mathbfcal{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
, Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d+m})
.

2:

Compute
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{0}={\rm{SVD}}_{r_{1}}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}))
.

3:

for

k=2,\ldots,d+m

do

4:

Compute
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{0}={\rm{SVD}}_{r_{k}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times^{k-1}_{l=1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{0}}))
.

5:

end

for

6:

Output
:
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{Y}}}={\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{0}}
.

Different from the low-rank matrix projection, which can be efficiently and exactly computed via truncated SVD, performing low-rank tensor projection exactly, even for
\mathbf{r}=1
, can be NP-hard in general. We thus introduce the following quasi-projection property and the approximation constant
\delta(d+m)
.

Definition 3
(Quasi-projection of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}
and Approximation Constant
\delta(d+m)
)

Let
P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}(\cdot)
be the projection map from
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
to the tensor space of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}:=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d+m})
, i.e., for any
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
and
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{Z}}}
of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}
, one always has
\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}-\widehat{{\mathbfcal{Z}}}\|_{{\rm F}}\geq\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}-P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}
.

We say
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}
satisfies the quasi-projection property with approximation constant
\delta(d+m)
if
\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\delta(d+m)\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}-P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}
for any
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
.

It is known that T-HOSVD and ST-HOSVD satisfy the
quasi-projection property
(Chapter 10 in
Hackbusch, 2012
).

Proposition 3
(Quasi-projection property of T-HOSVD and ST-HOSVD)

T-HOSVD and ST-HOSVD satisfy the quasi-projection property with the approximation constant
\delta(d+m)=\sqrt{d+m}
. That is for any
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
,

\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}-\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\sqrt{d+m}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}-P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}.

Here
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}
is either T-HOSVD or ST-HOSVD.

We note in the matrix setting by taking the rank
r
truncated SVD as the retraction operator, the approximation constant in Definition
3
is
1
, and this fact is used in Corollary
1
.

B
Additional Notation and Preliminaries

Let
\mathbb{N}=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
be the set of natural numbers. For any
{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p,r}
, we use
{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\in\mathbb{O}_{p,p-r}
to represent the orthonormal complement of
{\mathbf{U}}
. The matricization operation
\mathcal{M}_{k}(\cdot)
unfolds an order-
d
tensor along mode
k
to a matrix, say
{\mathbfcal{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
to
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{A}})\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{k}\times p_{-k}}
, where
p_{-k}=\prod_{j\neq k}p_{j}
. Specifically,
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{A}})\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{k}\times p_{-k}},

\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{A}})\right)_{\left[i_{k},j\right]}={\mathbfcal{A}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]},\quad j=1+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq k\end{subarray}}^{d}\left\{(i_{l}-1)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}z=1\\
z\neq k\end{subarray}}^{l-1}p_{z}\right\}

(20)

for any
1\leq i_{l}\leq p_{l},l=1,\ldots,d
. We also denote
\mathcal{T}_{k}(\cdot)
as the mode-
k
tensorization, i.e., the reverse operator of
\mathcal{M}_{k}(\cdot)
:
\mathcal{T}_{k}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{A}}))={\mathbfcal{A}}
for any
k=1,\ldots,d
The mode-
k
product of
{\mathbfcal{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
with a matrix
{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times p_{k}}
, denoted by
{\mathbfcal{A}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{B}}
, is a
p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{k-1}\times r_{k}\times p_{k+1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}
-dimensional tensor, defined as

({\mathbfcal{A}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{B}})_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{k-1},j,i_{k+1},\ldots,i_{d}]}=\sum_{i_{k}=1}^{p_{k}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{[i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{d}]}{\mathbf{B}}_{[j,i_{k}]}.

(21)

The inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
of any two tensors is defined as
\langle{\mathbfcal{A}},{\mathbfcal{B}}\rangle=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}{\mathbfcal{B}}_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]}
. The following equality connects the tensor-matrix product and matricizations
(
Kolda, 2001
, Section 4)
:

\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{k}\left({\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times\cdots\times_{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{d}\right)={\mathbf{U}}_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}})({\mathbf{U}}_{d}^{\top}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}^{\top}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k-1}^{\top}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}),\end{split}

(22)

where “
\otimes
” is the matrix Kronecker product. Recall the contracted tensor inner product is defined as follows

\left(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\rangle_{*}\right)_{[j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{l}=1,\\
l=1,\ldots,d\end{subarray}}^{p_{l}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i[k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}]}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}_{[k_{1},\ldots,k_{d},j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}.

(23)

Next, we provide the explicit formulas for the tangent space of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
at
{\mathbfcal{X}}

(
Koch and Lubich, 2010
;
Luo and Zhang, 2021
)
:

\begin{split}&T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}=\left\{{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\mathcal{T}_{k}({\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{\top}):\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbfcal{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{k}-r_{k})\times r_{k}},\\
k=1,\ldots,(d+m)\end{array}\right\}\\
=&\left\{{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}:\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathbfcal{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{k}-r_{k})\times r_{k}},\\
k=1,\ldots,d+m\end{array}\right\},\end{split}

(24)

where
\mathcal{T}_{k}(\cdot)
is the mode-
k
tensorization operator and
{\mathbf{W}}_{k}
is given in (
4
).

C
Rank Selection

To ensure a small estimation error of RGD and RGN, it is important to select a parsimonious rank
\mathbf{r}
, while also ensuring that
\mathbf{r}\geq\mathbf{r}^{*}
. In this section, we further introduce a data-driven method for choosing
\mathbf{r}
. Suppose
n
is even for simplicity of presentation. One straightforward idea is to use the first half of the samples
\{{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n/2}
to get an estimator of
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
under different input rank and then compute the prediction error of the estimator on the second half of the data. Then we can choose the rank that minimizes the out-of-sample prediction error.

In the rest of this section, we explore the empirical performance of this method in the scalar-on-tensor regression setting considered in Section
8.1
with
p=30,r^{*}=5,\sigma\in\{0.01,0.1,1\}
and
n=5000
. We consider different input ranks in
\{2,\ldots,9\}
and let
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}_{r}
denote the estimator returned by applying RGD or RGN on the first half of the data with input rank
r
. In the following Figure
9
, we plot the our-of-sample prediction error of the estimator
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}_{r}
on the holdout data, i.e.,
\sqrt{\sum_{i=n/2+1}^{n}({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}_{r}\rangle_{*})^{2}}
, versus different input rank
r
. We can see that the out-of-sample prediction error consistently achieves its minimum at the true rank value.

(a)
RGD

(b)
RGN

Fig 9:
Out-of-sample prediction error with different input rank in training. Here
p=30,r^{*}=5,n=5000,\sigma\in\{0.01,0.1,1\},r\in\{2,3,4,5,6,7,8,9\}
.

D
Proofs in Section
2

D.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Since
\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
is an embedded submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
and the Euclidean gradient of
f
in the ambient space is
\nabla f({\mathbfcal{X}})=\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}})-{\mathbfcal{Y}})
, the result follows from
(
Absil et al., 2009
, (3.37))
.
\blacksquare

D.2
Proof of Lemma
2

In our objective (
3
), the RGN update
\eta^{{\rm RGN}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
should solve the following RGN equation
(
Absil et al., 2009
, Chapter 8.4)
,

-{\rm grad\,}f({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})=P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\left(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}(\eta^{{\rm RGN}}))\right).

(25)

In view of the Riemannian Gauss-Newton equation in (
25
) and the Riemannian gradient in Lemma
1
, to prove the claim, we only need to show

P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}+\eta^{{\rm RGN}})-{\mathbfcal{Y}}))=0.

(26)

From the optimality condition of the least squares problem
\min_{\eta\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}+\eta)\|_{2}^{2}
, we have

P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\left(\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}+\eta^{{\rm RGN}})-{\mathbfcal{Y}}\right)=P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\left(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}+\eta^{{\rm RGN}})-{\mathbfcal{Y}}\right)=0.

This finishes the proof.
\blacksquare

E
Proofs in Section
3

We begin by introducing a few preliminary results and then give the proof in subsections. The following lemma shows that any tensor in
T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
is at most Tucker rank
2\mathbf{r}

Lemma 7

[
(
Luo and Zhang, 2021
, Lemma 1)
] For any
{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
, any tensor
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
is at most Tucker rank
2\mathbf{r}
.

The following lemma provides a user-friendly perturbation bound for singular subspaces under perturbation and is critical in our analysis.

Lemma 8

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a rank
r
matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
with economic singular value decomposition (SVD)
{\mathbf{U}}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
. Suppose
{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{A}}+{\mathbf{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
for some perturbation matrix
{\mathbf{Z}}
, and the top
r
truncated SVD of
{\mathbf{B}}
is given as
\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}
. Then

\max\left\{\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|,\|\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{V}}{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})\|\right\}\leq\frac{2\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|}{\sigma_{r}({\mathbf{A}})}.

Proof
. First, notice
\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\|=\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}\|=\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}},{\mathbf{U}})\|
by
(
Golub and Van Loan, 2013
, Theorem 2.5.1)
and
(
Cai and Zhang, 2018
, Lemma 1)
. This lemma is a special case of
(
Luo et al., 2021
, Theorem 5)
.

\blacksquare

Also for
{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
and the projector
P_{T_{\mathbfcal{X}}}(\cdot)
in (
5
), we let
P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}})_{\perp}}({\mathbfcal{Z}}):={\mathbfcal{Z}}-P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})
be the orthogonal complement of the projector
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}
.
The next lemma provides another useful upper bound which will be used frequently in our proof.

Lemma 9

{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}
is a Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}
tensor and
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
is a Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}^{*}
tensor. Then
\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}(P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}\leq R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}
.

Proof
.

\begin{split}\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}(P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}=&\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq 1}\langle P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}(P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}),{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle\\
=&\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq 1}\langle\mathscr{A}(P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}),\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\rangle\\
\overset{(a)}{\leq}&\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq 1}R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(b)}{\leq}&R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}.\end{split}

Here (a) is due to Lemma
13
,
\langle P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*},P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\rangle=0
,
P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
and
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})
are of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}^{*}
and
2\mathbf{r}
, respectively; (b) is because
\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq 1
.

\blacksquare

Next, we begin the proof for the results in Section
3
one by one.

E.1
Proof of Proposition
1

To prove the statement, we need to show when
n\geq C(\sum_{i=1}^{d}(p_{i}-r_{i})r_{i}+\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i})\log(d)/R^{2}_{\mathbf{r}}
, for any tensor
{\mathbfcal{Z}}
of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}
, we have
(1-R_{\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}\leq\|\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq(1+R_{\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}
holds with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c(\sum_{i=1}^{d}p_{i}))
.

Denote
{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}:={\mathbfcal{Z}}_{[:,\ldots,:,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{d+1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
for any
j_{1}\in[p_{d+1}],\cdots,j_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]
. Since
{\mathbfcal{Z}}
is of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}
and
{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
can be rewritten as
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{d+1}{\mathbf{e}}_{j_{1}}^{\top}\cdots\times_{d+m}{\mathbf{e}}^{\top}_{j_{m}}
where
{\mathbf{e}}_{i}
denotes the standard
i
-th base vector, we have
{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
is of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{d})
. Let us define another linear map
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}^{p_{d+1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}({\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})})_{[i]}=\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}\rangle
. It is easy to check
\|\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}=\sum_{j_{1}\in[p_{d+1}]}\cdots\sum_{j_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]}\|\mathscr{A}^{\prime}({\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})})\|_{2}^{2}
.

On the other hand, following the same proof of
(
Rauhut et al., 2017
, Theorem 2)
by replacing their Lemma 2 with a tighter covering number for the low Tucker rank tensor space given in Lemma
15
, we have when
n\geq C(\sum_{i=1}^{d}(p_{i}-r_{i})r_{i}+\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i})\log(d)/R^{2}_{\mathbf{r}}
, then with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c(\sum_{i=1}^{d}p_{i}))
,
(1-R_{\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{\prime}\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}\leq\|\mathscr{A}^{\prime}({\mathbfcal{Z}}^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}\leq(1+R_{\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{\prime}\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}
holds for any tensor
{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{\prime}
of Tucker rank at most
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}
. So

\begin{split}(1-R_{\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|^{2}_{\rm F}&=(1-R_{\mathbf{r}})\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}}\|\mathscr{A}^{\prime}({\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})})\|_{2}^{2}\\
&=\|\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\leq(1+R_{\mathbf{r}})\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&=(1+R_{\mathbf{r}})\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|^{2}_{\rm F},\end{split}

holds with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c(\sum_{i=1}^{d}p_{i}))
. This finishes the proof of this proposition.
\blacksquare

E.2
Proof of Theorem
1

First, notice the convergence result in the noiseless setting follows easily from the noisy setting by setting
{\mathbfcal{E}}=0
. We prove the convergence result in the noisy case. Recall
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-\alpha_{t}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})-{\mathbfcal{Y}})
where
\alpha_{t}=\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{A}}^{*}({\mathbfcal{A}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})-{\mathbfcal{Y}}))\|_{\rm F}^{2}/\|\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{A}}^{*}({\mathbfcal{A}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})-{\mathbfcal{Y}}))\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}
and
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+1}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})
. Then

\begin{split}&\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
=&\|\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\leq&\|\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\|_{{\rm F}}+\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(a)}{\leq}&\sqrt{d+m}\|P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\|_{{\rm F}}+\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(b)}{\leq}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
=&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-\alpha_{t}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})-{\mathbfcal{Y}})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(c)}{=}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-\alpha_{t}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})-{\mathbfcal{E}})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(d)}{\leq}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\Big(\underbrace{\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}}_{(A1)}+\underbrace{\|(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}-\alpha_{t}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}}_{(A2)}\\
&+\alpha_{t}\underbrace{\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}}_{(A3)}+\alpha_{t}\underbrace{\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}})\|_{\rm F}}_{(A4)}\Big).\end{split}

(27)

here (a) is by the quasi-projection property of T-HOSVD and ST-HOSVD in Proposition
3
; (b) is by the projection property of
P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}(\cdot)
; (c) is because
{\mathbfcal{Y}}=\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})+{\mathbfcal{E}}
; (d) is by triangle inequality.

Next, we bound
(A1),(A2),(A3)
and
(A4)
separately.

•

(A1)=\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}=\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: orthogonal projection}}{\leq}\frac{2(d+m)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}}{\underline{\lambda}}
.

•

\begin{split}(A2)\leq&\|(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}-\alpha_{t}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})\|\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\leq&\left(\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}-P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\|+|1-\alpha_{t}|\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\|\right)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(a)}{\leq}&(R_{2\mathbf{r}}+\frac{R_{2\mathbf{r}}}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}}))\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
=&\frac{2R_{2\mathbf{r}}}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}},\end{split}

where
(a)
is because
\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\|\leq 1+R_{2\mathbf{r}}
by Lemma
5
,
(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}})^{-1}\leq\alpha_{t}\leq(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})^{-1}\Longrightarrow|1-\alpha_{t}|\leq R_{2\mathbf{r}}/(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})
by the TRIP assumption on
\mathscr{A}
, and

\begin{split}\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}-P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\|&=\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{\rm F}=1}|\langle(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}-P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})({\mathbfcal{Z}}),{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle|\\
&=\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{\rm F}=1}\left|\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\|\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\right|\\
&\overset{(a^{\prime})}{\leq}R_{2\mathbf{r}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{\rm F}=1}\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq R_{2\mathbf{r}},\end{split}

(28)

where
(a^{\prime})
is by the TRIP assumption on
\mathscr{A}
.

•

(A3)=\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{{\rm F}}\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: orthogonal-projection-bound}}{\leq}R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: orthogonal projection}}{\leq}\frac{2(d+m)R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}}{\underline{\lambda}}
.

•

(A4)\leq\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
since
\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}})\|_{\rm F}
lies in
T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
and is of rank at most
2\mathbf{r}
by Lemma
7
.

By plugging upper bounds of
(A1)-(A4)
into (
27
), we have

\begin{split}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}&\leq\frac{2(\sqrt{d+m}+1)}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\left(R_{2\mathbf{r}}+\frac{(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})(d+m)}{\underline{\lambda}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\right)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
&\quad+\frac{\sqrt{d+m}+1}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}.\end{split}

(29)

Next, based on (
29
), we show inductively that for all
t\geq 0
, (
9
) and
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\frac{R_{2\mathbf{r}}}{(d+m)(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})}\underline{\lambda}
hold. First, it is clear the statements are true when
t=0
. Suppose now (
9
) and
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\frac{R_{2\mathbf{r}}}{(d+m)(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})}\underline{\lambda}
hold when
t=t_{0}
. Then

\begin{split}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}&\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{4R_{2\mathbf{r}}(\sqrt{d+m}+1)}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+\frac{\sqrt{d+m}+1}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
&\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+\frac{\sqrt{d+m}+1}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
&\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{2^{t_{0}+1}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+\frac{2(\sqrt{d+m}+1)}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}.\end{split}

(30)

Here (a) is based on (
29
) and the inductive assumption; (b) is because
\frac{4R_{2\mathbf{r}}(\sqrt{d+m}+1)}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\leq 1/2
based on the assumption
R_{2\mathbf{r}}\leq\frac{1}{8(\sqrt{d+m}+1)+1}
and (c) is based on the inductive assumption.

Finally since
\underline{\lambda}\geq\frac{2(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})(\sqrt{d+m}+1)(d+m)}{R_{2\mathbf{r}}(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
, and
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\frac{R_{2\mathbf{r}}}{(d+m)(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})}\underline{\lambda}
, we have

\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\overset{\eqref{ineq: RGD-induction}}{\leq}\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+\frac{\sqrt{d+m}+1}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\frac{R_{2\mathbf{r}}}{(d+m)(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})}\underline{\lambda}.

This ends the induction and also finishes the proof of this theorem.

\blacksquare

E.3
Proof of Theorem
2

Similar to the proof of Theorem
1
, we just need to prove the convergence result in the noisy case.

First, the least squares in (
8
) can be viewed as an unconstrained least squares in the vector space
T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
and by Lemma
5
, we know
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}
can be compactly written as
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}=(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})^{-1}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Y}})
and it is unique. So

\begin{split}&\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
=&\|\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\leq&\|\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{r}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\|_{{\rm F}}+\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(a)}{\leq}&\sqrt{d+m}\|P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\|_{{\rm F}}+\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(b)}{\leq}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
=&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\|(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})^{-1}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})+{\mathbfcal{E}})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
=&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\|(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})^{-1}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}(P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})\perp}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})+{\mathbfcal{E}})-P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})\perp}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\leq&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\left(\|(P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}})^{-1}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}(P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})\perp}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})+{\mathbfcal{E}})\|_{\rm F}+\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})\perp}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\right)\\
\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: spectral norm bound of Atop A}}{\leq}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\left(\frac{1}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\left(\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}(P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})\perp}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}+\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\right)+\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})\perp}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\right)\\
\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: orthogonal-projection-bound},\ref {lm: orthogonal projection}}{\leq}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\left(\underbrace{\frac{2(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})(d+m)}{(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})\underline{\lambda}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}_{(A1)}+\underbrace{\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}/(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})}_{(A2)}\right),\end{split}

(31)

here (a) is by the quasi-projection property of T-HOSVD and ST-HOSVD in Proposition
3
; (b) is by the projection property of
P_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}(\cdot)
.

Denote
\Delta:=\sqrt{\frac{\underline{\lambda}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}}{2(d+m)(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})}}
.
Notice, if
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\geq\Delta
, (A1) dominates (A2) and if
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\Delta
, (A2) dominates (A1).

By (
31
), when
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\geq\Delta
we have the error shrinks in each iteration as

\begin{split}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\frac{4(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})(d+m)}{(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})\underline{\lambda}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(32)

We show by induction that when
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\geq\Delta
,
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq 2^{-2^{t}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}
. This is true when
t=0
. Suppose it holds when
t=t_{0}
, then

\begin{split}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\overset{\eqref{ineq: quadratic-shrinkage}}{\leq}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\frac{4(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})(d+m)}{(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})\underline{\lambda}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{0}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\overset{(a)}{\leq}&(\sqrt{d+m}+1)\frac{4(1+R_{2\mathbf{r}+\mathbf{r}^{*}}-R_{2\mathbf{r}})(d+m)}{(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})\underline{\lambda}}\cdot 2^{-2^{t_{0}+1}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}\\
\overset{(b)}{\leq}&2^{-2^{t_{0}+1}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}},\end{split}

where (a) is by the inductive assumption and (b) is due to the initialization condition. This finishes the induction.

When
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\Delta
, the iteration error satisfies
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+1}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq\frac{2(\sqrt{d+m}+1)}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
. Combining two phases, we have

\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq 2^{-2^{t}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+\frac{2(\sqrt{d+m}+1)}{1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}},\quad\forall t\geq 0.

\blacksquare

E.4
Proof of Theorem
3

First, simple computation yields

\begin{split}0&\geq\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&=2\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})),{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle+\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}),\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\rangle\\
&=2\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})),{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle+\|\mathscr{A}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(33)

Since
\mathscr{A}
satisfies
2\mathbf{r}
-TRIP, we have

\begin{split}(1-R_{2\mathbf{r}})\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\|\mathscr{A}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}^{2}&\overset{\eqref{ineq: likelihood-compare}}{\leq}2\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})),\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\rangle\\
&=2\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\rangle\\
&=2\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),\frac{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}{\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}}\rangle\\
&\overset{(a)}{\leq}2\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}},\end{split}

(34)

here (a) is because
\frac{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}{\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}}
is of Tucker rank at most
2\mathbf{r}
and Frobenius norm at most
1
and by definition
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}
is equal to
\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}:{\rm Tucrank}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\leq 2\mathbf{r},\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}})\rangle
. The result follows from (
34
).
\blacksquare

E.5
Proof of Theorem
4

We prove the two statements in Part 1 and Part 2 separately.

Part 1
. The proof is based on a
\epsilon
-net argument. Let us first bound
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
. Recall

\begin{split}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}&:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}\end{subarray}}\|\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}})\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}\\
&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbfcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\\
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}\end{subarray}}\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\rangle\\
&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbfcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\\
{\mathbf{Q}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{k}\times r_{k}},\|{\mathbf{Q}}_{k}\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{Q}}_{k}\right\rangle\end{split}

(35)

Since
{\mathbfcal{E}}_{i}
has i.i.d.
N(0,\frac{\sigma^{2}}{n})
entries, for any fixed
{\mathbfcal{S}}
with
\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1
, and
\{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{d+m}
, condition on
\mathscr{A}
, we have

\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\rangle\Big|\mathscr{A}=\left\langle{\mathbfcal{E}},\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\right\rangle\Big|\mathscr{A}\sim N(0,\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma^{2}}{n}),

where
\tau^{2}=\|\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\|_{\rm F}^{2}
. Thus, by the tail bound for the Gaussian random variable (see
(
Wainwright, 2019
, Chapter 2.1.2)
), we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\rangle\geq t\Big|\mathscr{A}\right)\leq{\rm{exp}}(-\frac{nt^{2}}{2\tau^{2}\sigma^{2}}).\end{split}

(36)

When
n\geq C(\sum_{i=1}^{d}(p_{i}-r_{i})r_{i}+\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i})\log(d)
, by Proposition
1
,
\mathscr{A}
satisfies TRIP with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})
and denote the event that
\mathscr{A}
satisfies the TRIP property as
A
. So under
A
,
\tau^{2}\leq c\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq c
for some
c>0
and

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\rangle\geq t\Big|A\right)\leq{\rm{exp}}(-\frac{nt^{2}}{2c\sigma^{2}}).

(37)

By
(
Zhang and Xia, 2018
, Lemma 7)
, we can construct a
\epsilon
-net (
0<\epsilon<1
)
\{{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(1)},\cdots,{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(N_{{\mathbfcal{S}}})}\}
for
\{{\mathbfcal{S}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}}:\|{\mathbfcal{S}}^{\prime}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\}
such that

\sup_{{\mathbfcal{S}}^{\prime}:\|{\mathbfcal{S}}^{\prime}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\min_{i\leq N_{{\mathbfcal{S}}}}\|{\mathbfcal{S}}^{\prime}-{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i)}\|_{\rm F}\leq\epsilon

with
N_{{\mathbfcal{S}}}\leq(\frac{3}{\epsilon})^{\prod_{i=1}^{d+m}r_{i}}
.

At the same time, by
(
Szarek, 1982
, Proposition 8)
, for each
k=1,\ldots,d+m
, we can construct a
\epsilon
-net
\{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(N_{k})}\}
on the Grassmann manifold of
r_{k}
-dimensional subspaces in
\mathbb{R}^{p_{k}}
with the metric
d({\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2})=\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}\|
such that

\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}}\min_{i\leq N_{k}}d({\mathbf{U}}_{k},{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(i)})\leq\epsilon

with
N_{k}\leq(\frac{c_{0}}{\epsilon})^{r_{k}(p_{k}-r_{k})}
for some absolute constant
c_{0}>0
.

Suppose

({\mathbfcal{S}}^{*},\{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}\}_{k=1}^{d+m})=\mathop{\rm arg\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbfcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\\
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}\end{subarray}}\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\rangle

and denote
T:=\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{*}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{*}_{k}\right\rangle=\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
.
For
k\in[d+m]
, we can find
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(i_{k})}
in the corresponding
\epsilon
-net such that
d({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(i_{k})},{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*})\leq\epsilon
. Let
{\mathbf{O}}_{k}=\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{O}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(i_{k})}\|
. By
(
Cai and Zhang, 2018
, Lemma 1)
, we have
\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{O}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(i_{k})}\|\leq\sqrt{2}d({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(i_{k})},{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*})\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon
. Denote
\widebar{{\mathbfcal{S}}}={\mathbfcal{S}}^{*}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{O}}_{k}^{\top}
and let
{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}
be the one in the core tensor
\epsilon
-net such that
\|{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}-\widebar{{\mathbfcal{S}}}\|_{\rm F}\leq\epsilon
. Thus

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),\widebar{{\mathbfcal{S}}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{*}_{k}{\mathbf{O}}_{k}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),(\widebar{{\mathbfcal{S}}}-{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})})\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{*}_{k}{\mathbf{O}}_{k}\right\rangle\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{j<k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{(i_{j})}\times_{k}({\mathbf{U}}^{*}_{k}{\mathbf{O}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k})\times_{j>k}{\mathbf{U}}^{*}_{j}{\mathbf{O}}_{j}\right\rangle\\
\leq&(\sqrt{2}(d+m)+1)\epsilon T.\end{split}

(38)

So for
\epsilon<1/(2(\sqrt{2}(d+m)+1))
we have

\begin{split}T&=\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle+T-\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle\\
&=\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),\widebar{{\mathbfcal{S}}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{*}_{k}{\mathbf{O}}_{k}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq: telescoping}}{\leq}\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle+T/2.\end{split}

This implies
T\leq 2\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle
.

Then by union bound, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(T\geq t|A\right)&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{i^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,i^{\prime}_{d+m}}\left\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i^{\prime}_{0})}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}^{(i^{\prime}_{k})}_{k}\right\rangle\geq t/2\Big|A\right)\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq: single-point-Gaussian-upper}}{\leq}\left(N_{{\mathbfcal{S}}}\prod_{i=1}^{d+m}N_{i}\right){\rm{exp}}(-\frac{nt^{2}}{2c\sigma^{2}})={\rm{exp}}(C\cdot df-nt^{2}/(2c\sigma^{2})),\end{split}

where
df=\sum_{i=1}^{d+m}r_{i}(p_{i}-r_{i})+\prod_{i=1}^{d+m}r_{i}
. Notice here
C
will depend on
d,m
and for simplicity we omit them here. So we have
\mathbb{P}(T\geq c\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}\sigma|A)\leq{\rm{exp}}(-c^{\prime}\cdot df)
. Overall,

\mathbb{P}(T\leq c\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}\sigma)\geq\mathbb{P}(A)\mathbb{P}(T\leq t|A)\geq(1-{\rm{exp}}(-c^{\prime}\cdot df))(1-{\rm{exp}}(-c_{1}\underline{p}))\geq 1-{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p}).

Now to bound
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
, we just need to replace
\mathbf{r}
by
2\mathbf{r}
in the bound for
T
and up to a constant (depending on
d
and
m
only), the same upper bound for
T
holds for
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
.

Combining the upper bound of
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}
with Theorem
3
, we have given event
A
,
\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\geq ct
happens with probability at most
{\rm{exp}}(-nt^{2}/(2c\sigma^{2}))
for
t\geq c^{\prime}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}
. Thus

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}(\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}|A)\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\rm F}|A)}\\
=&\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\rm F}\geq t|A)}dt\\
=&\sqrt{\int_{0}^{a}\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\rm F}\geq t|A)dt+\int_{a}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\rm F}\geq t|A)dt}\\
\leq&\sqrt{a+\int_{a}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\geq\sqrt{t}|A)dt}\\
\overset{\text{let }a=c^{{}^{\prime}2}\sigma^{2}\frac{df}{n}}{\leq}&\sqrt{a+\int^{\infty}_{a}{\rm{exp}}(-nt/(2c\sigma^{2}))dt}\\
\leq&C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}.\end{split}

So
\mathbb{E}(\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F})\leq C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}
. This finishes the proof for part 1.

Part 2.
Suppose we can find a set of
\{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{r}}
such that
\min_{i\neq j}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{j}\|_{\rm F}\geq s
, by the standard argument of reducing the problem of providing a minimax risk lower bound to lower bounding the probability of error in a multiple hypothesis testing problem
(
Tsybakov, 2009
, Chapter 2)
, we have

\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{r}}}\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{s}{2}\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\neq{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}).

(39)

We will use Fano’s Lemma to lower bound the right-hand side of (
39
). Before that, let us first compute the Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence between two different distributions in our setting. For
i=1,\ldots,N
, let
\mathbb{Q}^{i}
denotes the conditional distribution of
{\mathbfcal{Y}}
given
\mathscr{A}
and
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}
. Suppose
{\mathbfcal{Y}}\sim\mathbb{Q}^{i}
, for
j_{1}\in[p_{d+1}],\ldots,j_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]
, let
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}:=({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[1,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{[n,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
{\mathbfcal{E}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
be defined in the same way. In addition, let
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}:={\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}_{[:,\ldots,:,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
and define
\mathscr{A}^{\prime}:\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
(\mathscr{A}^{\prime}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{i(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}))_{[k]}=\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{k}\rangle
where
\{{\mathbfcal{A}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}
are the same set of tensor covariates given in
\mathscr{A}
. Thus, we have
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}=\mathscr{A}^{\prime}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{i(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})})+{\mathbfcal{E}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
and denote
\mathbb{Q}^{i(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
as the conditional distribution of
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
condition on
\mathscr{A}
and
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}
. Condition on
\mathscr{A}
,
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
are independent for different
(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})
s. Due to the Gaussian ensemble design we assume, i.e.,
{\mathbfcal{E}}_{i}
has i.i.d.
N(0,\sigma^{2}/n)
entries, and the linear regression model we have on
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}
, the KL divergence between
\mathbb{Q}^{i_{1}}
and
\mathbb{Q}^{i_{2}}
, denoted by
{\rm{KL}}(\mathbb{Q}^{i_{1}}||\mathbb{Q}^{i_{2}})
, can be computed as follows

\begin{split}{\rm{KL}}(\mathbb{Q}^{i_{1}}||\mathbb{Q}^{i_{2}})&=\sum_{j_{1}\in[p_{d+1}]}\cdots\sum_{j_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]}{\rm{KL}}(\mathbb{Q}^{i_{1}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}||\mathbb{Q}^{i_{2}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})})\\
&=\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{n}{2\sigma^{2}}\left\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\right\rangle^{2}.\end{split}

(40)

Then by the Fano’s Lemma, e.g., see
(
Wainwright, 2019
, Chapter 15.3.2)
, we have

\begin{split}&~~\frac{s}{2}\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\neq{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i})=\frac{s}{2}\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\neq{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}|\mathscr{A}))\\
&\geq\frac{s}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{A}}\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}}(\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\neq{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}|\mathscr{A}))\\
&\geq\frac{s}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathscr{A}}\left(1-\frac{\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}{\rm{KL}}(\mathbb{Q}^{i_{1}}||\mathbb{Q}^{i_{2}})+\log 2}{\log N}\right)\\
&\overset{(a)}{=}\frac{s}{2}\left(1-\frac{\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}}\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}(j_{1},\ldots,j_{m})}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\log 2}{\log N}\right)\\
&=\frac{s}{2}\left(1-\frac{\frac{n}{2\sigma^{2}}\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\log 2}{\log N}\right),\end{split}

(41)

here (a) is because of (
40
) and
{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}
has i.i.d.
N(0,1/n)
entries.

Next, we consider two constructions for the sets of
\{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
so that we can have a proper lower bound for
\min_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\|_{\rm F}
and a proper upper bound for
\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\right\|_{\rm F}
.

Construction 1
. For
k=1,\ldots,d+m
, pick
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
. Given any
\delta>0
and
\min_{i}r_{i}\geq C^{\prime}
, by a slight modified version of
(
Agarwal et al., 2012
, Lemma 5)
, we can construct a set of
r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}
full Tucker rank tensors
\{{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(1)},\ldots,{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(N_{0})}\}
with cardinality
N_{0}\geq\frac{1}{4}{\rm{exp}}(\prod_{i=1}^{d+m}r_{i}/128)
such that: (1)
\|{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i)}\|_{\rm F}=\delta
holds for all
i=1,\ldots,N_{0}
, (2)
\|{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{2})}\|_{\rm F}\geq\delta
for all
i_{1},i_{2}\in[N_{0}],i_{1}\neq i_{2}
. Let
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}:={\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i)}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
for
i\in[N_{0}]
and clearly
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{r}}
.

For this set, we have

\begin{split}\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\right\|_{\rm F}&\leq\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}(\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}\|_{\rm F}+\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\right\|_{\rm F})=2\delta,\\
\min_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\|_{\rm F}&=\min_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\|({\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathbfcal{S}}^{(i_{2})})\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\rm F}\geq\delta.\end{split}

(42)

Plug in the results in (
42
) into (
39
) and (
41
), we have

\begin{split}\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{r}}}\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\left(1-\frac{2n\delta^{2}/\sigma^{2}+\log 2}{c_{1}\prod_{i=1}^{d+m}r_{i}}\right)\overset{(a)}{\geq}c\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{d+m}r_{i}}{n}}\sigma,\end{split}

(43)

where in (a) we pick
\delta^{2}
to be
c^{\prime}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{d+m}r_{i}}{n}\sigma^{2}
for some
c^{\prime}>0
.

Construction 2
. Let
{\mathbfcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}}
be a fixed core tensor of full Tucker rank such that

c\lambda\leq\min_{i}\sigma_{r_{i}}(\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{S}}))\leq\max_{i}\sigma_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{S}}))\leq C\lambda,

for some
c,C,\lambda>0
. For
k=1,\ldots,d+m
, pick
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
.

Let us first focus on mode-
1
. Consider the metric space
(\mathcal{G}_{p_{1},r_{1}},d(\cdot,\cdot))
, where
\mathcal{G}_{p_{1},r_{1}}
denotes the Grassmann manifold of
r_{1}
dimensional subspaces in
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}
and
d({\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2})=\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\rm F}
. Consider the following ball of radius
\epsilon>0
and centered at
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
:

B({\mathbf{U}}_{1},\epsilon)=\{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}}:d({\mathbf{U}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\leq\epsilon\}.

By
(
Cai et al., 2013
, Lemma 1)
, for
0<\alpha<1
and
0<\epsilon\leq 1
, there exists
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(N_{1})}\in B({\mathbf{U}}_{1},\epsilon)
such that
\min_{1\leq i<j\leq N_{1}}d({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(i)},{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(j)})\geq\alpha\epsilon
with
N_{1}\geq(c_{0}/\alpha)^{r_{1}(p_{1}-r_{1})}
for some absolute constant
c_{0}
.

Let
{\mathbf{O}}_{i}=\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{1}}}\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(i)}{\mathbf{O}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{\rm F}
and let
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(i)}{\mathbf{O}}_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,N_{1}
. By
(
Cai and Zhang, 2018
, Lemma 1)
, we have
\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{\rm F}\leq d({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(i)},{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\leq\epsilon
for all
i\in[N_{1}]
. We then construct
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i}={\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\times_{k=2}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
for
i\in[N_{1}]
. For this set, we have

\begin{split}\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\left\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\right\|_{\rm F}&\leq\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{2})})\times_{k=2}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\rm F}\\
&\leq\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}(\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{2})}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\times_{k=2}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\rm F}+\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\times_{k=2}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\rm F})\\
&\leq 2C\epsilon\lambda,\\
\min_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{1}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{i_{2}}\|_{\rm F}&=\min_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{2})})\times_{k=2}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\rm F}\\
&\overset{(a)}{\geq}\min_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\min_{{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{1}}}\|{\mathbfcal{S}}\times_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{2})}{\mathbf{O}})\times_{k=2}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{\rm F}\geq c\alpha\epsilon\lambda/\sqrt{2}.\end{split}

(44)

Here (a) is because
\min_{{\mathbf{O}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{1}}}\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{2})}{\mathbf{O}}\|_{\rm F}\geq d({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(i_{2})})/\sqrt{2}
by
(
Cai and Zhang, 2018
, Lemma 1)
and
d({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(i_{2})})=d({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}(i_{1})},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{{}^{\prime}(i_{2})})\geq\alpha\epsilon
.

Plug (
44
) into (
39
) and (
41
), we have

\begin{split}\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{r}}}\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{c\alpha\epsilon\lambda}{2\sqrt{2}}\left(1-\frac{Cn\epsilon^{2}\lambda^{2}/\sigma^{2}+\log 2}{\log(c_{0}/\alpha)r_{1}(p_{1}-r_{1})}\right)\overset{(a)}{\geq}c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}(p_{1}-r_{1})}{n}}\sigma,\end{split}

(45)

where in (a) we pick
\epsilon^{2}
to be
c^{\prime}\frac{r_{1}(p_{1}-r_{1})}{n\lambda^{2}}\sigma^{2}
,
\alpha=(c_{0}\wedge 1)/8
.

We can apply similar procedures on modes
2
to
(d+m)
and get

\begin{split}\inf_{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}\sup_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{r}}}\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}\|_{\rm F}\geq c\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}r_{k}(p_{k}-r_{k})}{n}}\sigma.\end{split}

(46)

Combining it with (
43
), we obtain the full minimax risk lower bound.
\blacksquare

F
Proofs in Section
4

F.1
Proof of Theorem
5

Denote
\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}=\|{\mathbfcal{A}}\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+\sigma^{2}
. We first check the condition required for applying Theorem
10
. Recall
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}={\rm{SVD}}_{r_{k}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}})))
and let
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}=[\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}\quad\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}]
where
\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
is composed of the first
r_{k}^{*}
columns of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
and
\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
contains the remaining
(r_{k}-r_{k}^{*})
columns of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
. By the proof of
(
Zhang et al., 2020
, Theorem 4, step 1)
, we have with probability at least
1-\underline{p}^{-C}
for some
C>0
such that when
n\geq c(d)(\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+\sigma^{2})\frac{(\prod_{i=1}^{d}p_{i})^{1/2}+\bar{p}}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}
, we have

\begin{split}&\left\|\sin\Theta(\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0},{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\right\|=\|\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{0\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{p_{k}/n}\tilde{\sigma}\underline{\lambda}+(\prod_{k=1}^{d}p_{k})^{1/2}\widetilde{\sigma}^{2}/n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}.\end{split}

(47)

Moreover,

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{0\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|=\|({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}}){\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|=\|({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}})({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}}){\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|\leq\|({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}}){\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|=\|\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{0\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}.\end{split}

(48)

Consider applying Theorem
10
in our setting, we have
{\mathbfcal{T}}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
,
\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}=\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}})
and
{\mathbfcal{Z}}=\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}=\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}+\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})
. With probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})
, we have

\begin{split}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}&=\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}},k=1,\ldots,d}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{k=1}^{d}P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
&=\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{k=1}^{d}P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\rangle\\
&=\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k=1}^{d}P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}}\rangle\\
