&\leq\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k=1}^{d}P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}}\rangle\\
&\quad+\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}),{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k=1}^{d}P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}}\rangle\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm:retricted orthogonal property},\text{Theorem }\ref {th: lower-bound}}{\leq}R_{2\mathbf{r}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}(p_{i}-r_{i})+\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}}{n}}\\
&\overset{\text{Proposition }\ref {prop: TRIP-under-Gaussian-design}}{\leq}C(\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+\sigma)\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}(p_{i}-r_{i})+\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}}{n}}.\end{split}

So when
n
satisfies the condition indicated in the proposition, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\underline{\lambda}
. Thus by Theorem
10
, we have the output of the algorithm satisfies

\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c^{\prime}(d)\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\underline{\lambda}

as long as
n\geq c(d)\left(\frac{(\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}+\sigma^{2})}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\left((\prod_{i=1}^{d}p_{i})^{1/2}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}(p_{i}-r_{i})r_{i}+\prod_{i=1}^{d}r_{i}\right)\right)
for sufficiently large
c(d)
.

Since
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
by Theorem
4
, when
\underline{\lambda}\leq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, the estimator is already optimal after initialization, no further refinement is needed. If
\underline{\lambda}\geq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, TRIP holds for
n
indicated in the proposition, then by Theorem
1
, after
t_{\max}\geq\log\left(\frac{\underline{\lambda}\sqrt{n/df}}{c_{1}(d)\sigma}\right)
, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{\max}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq c_{3}(d)\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}
. A similar argument applies to the study of RGN.

\blacksquare

F.2
Proof of Theorem
6

Since
{\mathbf{A}}
is a
n
-by-
p_{1}
matrix with i.i.d.
N(0,1/n)
entries and
n\geq Cp_{1}
, we have the
{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{A}}
factor of the QR decomposition of
{\mathbf{A}}
has the spectrum bound

1-c\leq\sigma_{\min}({\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{A}})\leq\sigma_{\max}({\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{A}})\leq 1+c

(49)

for some
1>c>0
with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c_{1}p_{1})
by the standard random matrix theory, see
(
Vershynin, 2010
, Corollary 5.35)
.

Let
\widebar{{\mathbfcal{Y}}}:={\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{A}}+\widebar{{\mathbfcal{E}}}
where
\widebar{{\mathbfcal{E}}}={\mathbfcal{E}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}
has i.i.d.
N(0,\sigma^{2}/n)
entries. Denote
\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{*}:={\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{A}}
, and its Tucker decomposition as
\widebar{{\mathbfcal{S}}}\times_{i=1}^{m+1}\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}
, we have
(1+c)\underline{\lambda}\geq\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}:=\min_{i}\sigma_{r_{i}^{*}}(\mathcal{M}_{i}(\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{*}))\geq(1-c)\underline{\lambda}
. Let
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}=[\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}\quad\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}]
where
\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
is composed of the first
r_{k}^{*}
columns of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
and
\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
contains the remaining
(r_{k}-r_{k}^{*})
columns of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}
. By the proof of Theorem 1 in
Zhang and Xia, 2018
, we have for the sample size indicated in the proposition, with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})
, the following inequalities hold for
k=1,\ldots,m+1
:

\begin{split}&\left\|\sin\Theta(\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0},{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\right\|=\|\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{0\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|\leq\frac{\sigma\sqrt{p_{k}}}{\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}\sqrt{n}}+\frac{(\prod_{k=1}^{m+1}p_{k})^{1/2}\sigma^{2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{{}^{\prime}2}n}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}.\end{split}

(50)

Moreover,

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{0\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|=\|({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}}){\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|=\|({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}})({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}}){\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|\leq\|({\mathbf{I}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{0}}){\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|=\|\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{0\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}.\end{split}

Consider applying Theorem
10
in our setting, we have
{\mathbfcal{T}}=\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{*}
,
\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}=\widebar{{\mathbfcal{Y}}}
and
{\mathbfcal{Z}}=\widebar{{\mathbfcal{Y}}}-\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{*}=\widebar{{\mathbfcal{E}}}
. Moreover, by a similar proof of Theorem
4
part I, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}=\|\widebar{{\mathbfcal{E}}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}
for some
C>0
with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p}).
So when
n
satisfies the condition indicated in the proposition, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}
. Thus by Theorem
10
, we have for the output of OHOOI,
\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{0}
, satisfies

\|\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{0}-\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c^{\prime}(d)\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c^{\prime}\underline{\lambda}^{\prime}\leq C^{\prime}\underline{\lambda}.

So
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}=\|(\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{0}-\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{*})\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}\|_{\rm F}\leq c\|\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{0}-\widebar{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq C^{\prime}\underline{\lambda}
.

Since
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}=\|({\mathbfcal{E}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}=\|(\widebar{{\mathbfcal{E}}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, when
\underline{\lambda}\leq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, the initialization is already optimal, no further refinement is needed. If
\underline{\lambda}\geq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, we consider the local refinement. By the spectral bounds in (
49
) for
{\mathbf{A}}
, following a similar proof of Proposition
1
, we have TRIP holds for
\mathscr{A}
holds with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-cp_{1})
when
n\geq Cp_{1}
. Then by Theorem
1
, after
t_{\max}\geq\log\left(\frac{\underline{\lambda}\sqrt{n/df}}{c_{1}(m)\sigma}\right)
, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{\max}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq c_{3}(m)\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}
. A similar argument applies to the study of RGN.
\blacksquare

F.3
Proof of Theorem
7

Let
{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times(r+r^{*})}
be the orthogonal matrix spans the column spaces of
{\mathbf{X}}^{0}
and
{\mathbf{X}}^{*}
. Since

\|{\mathbf{X}}^{0}-\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{X}}^{0}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0\perp}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}^{2}

and

\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\|P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0\perp}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}^{2},

the SVD property
\|{\mathbf{X}}^{0}-\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}
implies that

\|{\mathbf{X}}^{0}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

Note that following the same proof as (
28
), we have

\begin{split}\|P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}\|\leq R_{2r}.\end{split}

(51)

Hence,

\begin{split}\|{\mathbf{X}}^{0}-{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}&\leq\|{\mathbf{X}}^{0}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}+\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}\\
&\leq 2\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbf{y}}))\|_{\rm F}\\
&\overset{(a)}{=}2\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\mathscr{A}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))\|_{\rm F}\\
&=2\|P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}(P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*})-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))\|_{\rm F}\\
&\leq 2\left(\|(P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}){\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\|P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))\|_{\rm F}\right)\\
&\overset{(b)}{\leq}2R_{2r}\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+2\sqrt{2}\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))_{\max(r)}\|_{\rm F},\end{split}

(52)

where (a) is due to the model of
{\mathbf{y}}
and (b) is due to that
P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))
is a at most rank
2r
matrix and the spectral norm bound for the operator
(P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}-P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}})
in (
51
).

By Theorem
4
part I, we have
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))_{\max(r)}\|_{\rm F}\leq c^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r}{n}}\sigma
holds with probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})
. At the same time, by Proposition
1
, there exists
C>0
such that when
n\geq C(p_{1}+p_{2}-r)r\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\rm F}/\sigma^{2}_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})
, we have
R_{2r}\leq c_{1}\frac{\sigma_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})}{\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}}
. So for
n
indicated in the proposition, we have
\|{\mathbf{X}}^{0}-{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq c\sigma_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})
. Since
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))_{\max(r)}\|_{\rm F}\leq c^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}\sigma
, if
\sigma_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})\leq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, the estimator is already optimal after initialization, no further refinement is needed. If
\sigma_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})\geq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, then by Corollary
1
, after
t_{\max}\geq\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})\sqrt{n/df}}{c_{1}\sigma}\right)
, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{\max}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq c_{3}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}
. A similar argument applies to the study of RGN.

\blacksquare

F.4
Proof of Theorem
8

First, notice that in this special setting,
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{1}
and
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{2}
can be compactly represented as
{\mathbfcal{Y}}^{j}=\lambda\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{w}_{j}}{\|\mathbf{w}_{j}\|_{2}}\circ{\mathbf{u}}_{d+1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{u}}_{d+m}+{\mathbfcal{E}}^{j}
for
j=1
and
2
, where
{\mathbfcal{E}}^{1}
and
{\mathbfcal{E}}^{2}
collect the noise in the first and second half of the data and

\begin{split}&\mathbf{w}_{1}=(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{1},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{u}}_{d}\rangle,\cdots,\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{n},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{u}}_{d}\rangle)^{\top},\\
&\mathbf{w}_{2}=(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{u}}_{d}\rangle,\cdots,\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{2n},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{u}}_{d}\rangle)^{\top}.\end{split}

(53)

Notice that
\mathbf{w}_{1}
and
\mathbf{w}_{2}
consist i.i.d.
N(0,1/n)
random variables. So we have
n\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{2}/2\sim\chi_{n/2}^{2}
. Then for any
k=d+1,\ldots,d+m
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k\perp}\|\geq\sqrt{2}/2\right)\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k\perp}\|\geq\sqrt{2}/2,n\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}-n\geq-n/16\right)+\mathbb{P}(n\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}-n\leq-n/16)\\
\overset{(a)}{\leq}&\mathbb{P}\left(\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k\perp}\|\geq\sqrt{2}/2\Big|n\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}-n\geq-n/16\right)\mathbb{P}(n\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}-n\geq-n/16)+{\rm{exp}}(-cn)\\
\overset{(b)}{\leq}&{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})\mathbb{P}(n\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}-n\geq-n/16)+{\rm{exp}}(-cn)\\
\leq&C{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p}),\end{split}

where (a) is by the standard concentration for
\chi^{2}
-distribution; (b) is because given
n\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}-n\geq-n/16
, i.e.,
\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 15/16
, we have
\lambda\|\mathbf{w}_{1}\|_{2}/(\sigma/\sqrt{n})\geq C\left[(n\prod_{k=d+1}^{d+m}p_{i})^{1/4}+\sqrt{\bar{p}}+\sqrt{n}\right]
under the condition of
n\geq c(d,m)\left(\bar{p}+\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\lambda^{4}}\left(\prod^{d+m}_{i=d+1}p_{i}+\bar{p}\right)\right)
stated in the theorem and by the Step 1 in the proof of
(
Zhang and Xia, 2018
, Theorem)
. By union bound, we have
\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{k=d+1,\ldots,d+m}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k\perp}\|\geq\sqrt{2}/2\right)\leq Cm{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})
.

Now, we move onto the guarantee in the second step. Notice that condition on
\{\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0}\}_{k=d+1}^{d+m}
, we have

{\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{\prime}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}\lambda\prod_{k=d+1}^{d+m}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k})\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ{\mathbf{u}}_{d}\rangle+\epsilon_{i}^{\prime},\quad\text{ for }i=n/2+1,\ldots,n,

and
\epsilon_{i}^{\prime}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}N(\sigma^{2}/n)
. Moreover, with probability
1-Cm{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})
,
\prod_{k=d+1}^{d+m}|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k}|\asymp 1
. By the proof of
(
Zhang et al., 2020
, Theorem 4, step 1)
, we have with probability at least
1-\underline{p}^{-C}
for some
C>0
such that when
n\geq c(d)(\lambda^{2}+\sigma^{2})\frac{(\prod_{i=1}^{d}p_{i})^{1/2}+\bar{p}}{\lambda^{2}}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k\perp}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{p_{k}/n}\sqrt{\lambda^{2}+\sigma^{2}}\lambda+(\prod_{k=1}^{d}p_{k})^{1/2}(\lambda^{2}+\sigma^{2})/n}{\lambda^{2}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}.\end{split}

By the union bound, we have condition on
\{\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0}\}_{k=d+1}^{d+m}
and
\prod_{k=d+1}^{d+m}|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k}|\asymp 1
,
\max_{k=1,\ldots,d}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k\perp}\|\geq\sqrt{2}/2
holds with probability at most
Cdp^{-C^{\prime}}
. In summary, with probability at least
1-\underline{p}^{-C}
, we have
\max_{k=1,\ldots,d+m}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}}_{k}^{0\top}{\mathbf{u}}_{k\perp}\|\leq\sqrt{2}/2
.

Consider applying Theorem
10
in our setting, we have
{\mathbfcal{T}}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
,
\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}=\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Y}})
and
{\mathbfcal{Z}}=\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Y}})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}=\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}+\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}})
. With probability at least
1-{\rm{exp}}(-c\underline{p})
, we have

\begin{split}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}&=\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},1},k=1,\ldots,d+m}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}_{k}}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
&=\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},1},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}_{k}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\rangle\\
&=\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},1},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}_{k}}\rangle\\
&\leq\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},1},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}_{k}}\rangle\\
&\quad+\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},1},\|{\mathbfcal{W}}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\langle\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}),{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}_{k}}\rangle\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm:retricted orthogonal property},\text{Theorem }\ref {th: lower-bound}}{\leq}R_{2\mathbf{r}}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}+C\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{d+m}p_{i}}{n}}\\
&\overset{\text{Proposition }\ref {prop: TRIP-under-Gaussian-design}}{\leq}C(\lambda+\sigma)\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{d+m}p_{i}}{n}}.\end{split}

So when
n
satisfies the condition indicated in the proposition, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\underline{\lambda}
. Thus by Theorem
10
, we have the output of the algorithm satisfies

\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{0}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c^{\prime}(d)\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\lambda

with probability at least
1-\underline{p}^{-C}
.

Since
\|(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{E}}))_{\max(2\mathbf{r})}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq c\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
by Theorem
4
, when
\lambda\leq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, the estimator is already optimal after initialization, no further refinement is needed. If
\lambda\geq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{df/n}
, TRIP holds for
n
indicated in the proposition, then by Theorem
1
, after
t_{\max}\geq\log\left(\frac{\lambda\sqrt{n/df}}{c_{1}(d,m)\sigma}\right)
, we have
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t_{\max}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq c_{3}(d,m)\sigma\sqrt{\frac{df}{n}}
. A similar argument applies to the study of RGN.

G
Proofs in Section
5

G.1
An Equivalence Formulation For Scalar-on-tensor Regression

In this section, we show without loss of generality, we can assume
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}N(0,\sigma^{2})
with
0\leq\sigma^{2}<1
,
{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}N(0,1)
and
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}=1
in establishing the computational lower bound for scalar-on-tensor regression under Gaussian design. Consider (
10
) and suppose we are in a simpler setting that
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}
and
\sigma
are known. Then we can rescale the problem by multiplying
\sqrt{n/(\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2})}
on both sides of (
10
) and get

{\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{\prime}=\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}^{\prime},{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*^{\prime}}\rangle+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime},\quad i=1,\ldots,n,

(54)

where
{\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{\prime}=\sqrt{n}{\mathbf{y}}_{i}/\sqrt{\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}}\sim N(0,1)
,
{\mathbfcal{A}}^{\prime}=\sqrt{n}{\mathbfcal{A}}
has i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries and
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*^{\prime}}={\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}/\sqrt{\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}}
,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime}
follows i.i.d.
N(0,\sigma^{2}/\sqrt{\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}})
satisfying
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*^{\prime}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+{\textrm{V}ar}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\prime})=1
. It is not hard to see that any lower bound established for estimating
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*^{\prime}}
based on
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{\prime},{\mathbfcal{A}}^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, say
c_{\textrm{lower}}
, also implies the lower bound for estimating
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
. This is because for any estimator
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}
of
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
, we have

\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}=\sqrt{\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}}\left\|\frac{\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}}{\sqrt{\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*^{\prime}}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq c_{\textrm{lower}}\sqrt{\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}}.

Thus, without loss of generality, we can assume
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}N(0,\sigma^{2})
with
0\leq\sigma^{2}<1
,
{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}N(0,1)
and
\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\sigma^{2}=1
.

G.2
More Backgroud and Preliminaries for Low-degree Polynomials Method

Next, we provide a few preliminary facts for low-degree polynomials methods. Given data
X
, consider the simple hypothesis testing problem:
H_{0}
v.s.
H_{1}
. We have the following result for the low-degree likelihood ratio.

Proposition 4
(Page 35 of
Hopkins, 2018
or
Kunisky et al., 2019
Proposition 1.15)

Let likelihood ratio be
{\rm{LR}}(x)=\frac{p_{H_{1}}(x)}{p_{H_{0}}(x)}:\Omega^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
. For every
D\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\frac{{\rm{LR}}^{\leq D}-1}{\|{\rm{LR}}^{\leq D}-1\|}=\mathop{\rm arg\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}f:f\text{ has degree at most }D\\
\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}f^{2}(X)=1,\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}f(X)=0\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}f(X)

and

\|{\rm{LR}}^{\leq D}-1\|=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}f:f\text{ has degree at most }D\\
\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}f^{2}(X)=1,\\
\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}f(X)=0\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}f(X),

where
\|f\|=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}f^{2}(X)}
and
f^{\leq D}
is the projection of a function
f
to the linear subspace of degree-
D
polynomials, where the projection is orthonormal with respect to the inner product induced under
H_{0}
.

Here the key quantity we are interested to bound is
\|{\rm{LR}}^{\leq D}-1\|
. Suppose
D\geq 1
is fixed,
f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{q}:\Omega^{n}\to R
are orthonormal basis for degree
D
functions (with respect to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{H_{0}}
), and that
f_{0}(x)=1
is a constant function. Then by the property of basis functions, we have

\begin{split}\|{\rm{LR}}^{\leq D}-1\|^{2}&=\sum_{i=1}^{q}\left(\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}(f_{i}(X)({\rm{LR}}^{\leq D}-1))\right)^{2}\\
&\overset{(a)}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left(\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}(f_{i}(X){\rm{LR}}(X))\right)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{D}(\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}f_{i}(X))^{2},\end{split}

(55)

here (a) is because
{\rm{LR}}-({\rm{LR}}^{\leq D}-1)
is orthogonal to
f_{i}
for
i\in[1,D]
by assumption. So the typical main task in the low-degree polynomials method boils down to bound
\sum_{i=1}^{D}(\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}f_{i}(X))^{2}
. In the hypothesis testing formulation for scalar-on-tensor regression (
14
), the data are i.i.d. Gaussian under the null hypothesis. So a natural choice for the basis functions are Hermite polynomials, which are orthogonal polynomials with respect to the Gaussian measure
(
Szeg, 1939
)
.

Recall
\mathbb{N}=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
, let
\{h_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be the normalized univariate Hermite polynomials
h_{k}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{k!}}H_{k}
where
\{H_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are univariate Hermite polynomials which are defined by the following recurrence:

H_{0}(x)=1,\quad H_{1}(x)=x,\quad H_{k+1}(x)=xH_{k}(x)-kH_{k-1}(x)\quad\text{ for }k\geq 1.

The normalized univariate Hermite polynomials satisfy the following key property:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(0,1)}[h_{k}(Z)]=0\quad\forall k\geq 0,\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim N(0,1)}[h_{k_{1}}(Z)h_{k_{2}}(Z)]=1(k_{1}=k_{2}),\end{split}

where
1(\cdot)
is the indicator function. In addition, for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, let
h_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}({\mathbf{Z}})=\prod_{i\in[n]}h_{\alpha_{i}}(Z_{i})
be the normalized
n
-variate Hermite polynomials. They form an orthogonal basis with respect to
N(0,1)^{\otimes n}
, i.e., if
{\mathbf{Z}}
has i.i.d.
N(0,1)
entries, then
\mathbb{E}[h_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}({\mathbf{Z}})h_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}({\mathbf{Z}})]=1(\boldsymbol{\alpha}=\boldsymbol{\beta})
. Next, we introduce two additional properties regards Hermite polynomials.

Lemma 10

(Expansion for Shifted Hermite Polynomials (
(
Schramm and Wein, 2022
, Proposition 3.1)
)) For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
z,\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, then

H_{k}(z+\mu)=\sum_{l=0}^{k}{k\choose l}\mu^{k-l}H_{l}(z),

and

h_{k}(z+\mu)=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sqrt{\frac{l!}{k!}}{k\choose l}\mu^{k-l}h_{l}(z).

Lemma 11

(Gaussian Integration by Parts
(
Kunisky et al., 2019
, Proposition 2.10)
) If
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is a
k
(
k\geq 0
) times continuously differentiable and
f(y)
and its first
k
derivatives are bounded by
O({\rm{exp}}(|y|^{\alpha}))
for some
\alpha\in(0,2)
, then

\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim N(0,1)}[h_{k}(Y)f(Y)]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{k!}}\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim N(0,1)}\left[\frac{d^{k}f}{dY^{k}}(Y)\right].

G.3
Proof of Theorem
9

First, it is easy to check under
H_{1}
, we have

({\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\rm{vec}}({\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}))|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}N\left(0,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&({\rm{vec}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}))^{\top}\\
{\rm{vec}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})&{\mathbf{I}}_{p^{d}}\end{array}\right)\right).

(56)

Moreover, let us denote the distribution of
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
under
H_{1}
as
\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}
. Since the data is i.i.d. standard Gaussian under
H_{0}
, Hermite polynomials are a natural choice for the orthogonal polynomial basis under
H_{0}
. Let the degrees of
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
to be
\{\alpha_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
defined in the following way:

\boldsymbol{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n},\quad\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}^{np^{d}}=\{\beta_{i,{\mathbf{j}}}\}_{i\in[n],{\mathbf{j}}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{d})\in[p]^{\otimes d}},\quad\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}=\{\beta_{i,{\mathbf{j}}}\}_{{\mathbf{j}}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{d})\in[p]^{\otimes d}}.

Finally, throughout the proof, given any vector
{\mathbf{a}}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{q})\in\mathbb{R}^{q},{\mathbf{b}}=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{q})\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
, let
|{\mathbf{a}}|=\sum_{j=1}^{q}a_{j}
and
{\mathbf{a}}!=\prod_{j=1}^{q}a_{j}!
,
{\mathbf{b}}^{\mathbf{a}}=\prod_{j=1}^{q}b_{j}^{a_{j}}
.

By Proposition
4
and the properties of Hermite polynomials introduced in Appendix
G.2
, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{\text{polynomial }f:~\begin{subarray}{c}deg(f)\leq D\\
\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}f(\{y_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})=0,\\
\text{Var}_{H_{0}}f(\{y_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}f(\{y_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})=&\|\text{LR}^{\leq D}-1\|\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: evalutation-truncated-likelihood-ratio}}{=}&\sqrt{\sum_{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}):1\leq|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq D}\left(\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}h_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\right)^{2}}.\end{split}

(57)

Then

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}\left(h_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\right)\overset{(a)}{=}&\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}({\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i})|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\right)\right)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: joint-distribution-H1},\text{Lemma }\ref {lm:correlated-hermitian}}{=}&\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{i}!}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}!}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}1(\alpha_{i}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}|)\right),\end{split}

(58)

where (a) is because condition on
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
,
({\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i})
are independent.

We divide the rest of the proof into two steps.

Step 1
In this step, we bound the right-hand side of (
57
).

\begin{split}&\sum_{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}):|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq D}\left(\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}h_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\right)^{2}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: low-degree-poly-exp-com}}{=}&\sum_{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}):\alpha_{i}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}|,\forall i\in[n],|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha_{i}!}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}!}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}\right)\right)^{2}\\
\overset{(a)}{=}&\sum_{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}):\alpha_{i}=|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}|,\forall i\in[n],|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\alpha_{i}!}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}!}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}\right)\right)\\
=&\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\sum_{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}:|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{1}|=\alpha_{1}}\cdots\sum_{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}:|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{n}|=\alpha_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\alpha_{i}!}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}!}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}\right)\\
=&\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}:|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}|=\alpha_{i}}\frac{\alpha_{i}!}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}!}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}}\right)\right)\\
\overset{(b)}{=}&\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}_{1},{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}_{2}\rangle^{\alpha_{i}}\right)\\
\overset{(c)}{=}&\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\left((1-\sigma^{2})\langle{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{1},{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{2}\rangle^{d}\right)^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}\right).\end{split}

(59)

Here (a) is by performing the “replica” manipulation where
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*}
,
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*}
are drawn independently from
\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}
; (b) is by the sum of multinomials; (c) is by the generating process of
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*}
and
{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*}
.

Given
(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta})
, let
I_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=\{i\in[n]:\alpha_{i}\neq 0\}
and
|I_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|
be the cardinality of the set
I_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
. Also notice that since
{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{1}
and
{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{2}
has i.i.d. entries generated from Uniform
(1/\sqrt{p},-1/\sqrt{p})
, we have
p\langle{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{1},{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{2}\rangle
has i.i.d. Uniform
(1,-1)
entries. By the property of sub-gaussian random variable, for any integer
k\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{E}(|p\langle{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{1},{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{2}\rangle|^{k})\leq p^{k/2}k^{k/2}

(60)

by
(
Vershynin, 2010
, Eq. (5.11))
and the fact
p\langle{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{1},{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{2}\rangle
has sub-gaussian norm
\sqrt{p}
. Then we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}):|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq D}\left(\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}h_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\right)^{2}\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: low-degree-second-argument}}{\leq}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\frac{(1-\sigma^{2})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}}{p^{d|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbfcal{X}}_{2}^{*}\sim\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}}}\left(\left(|p\langle{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{1},{\mathbf{x}}^{*}_{2}\rangle|\right)^{d|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}\right)\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq: sub-gaussian-abs-bound}}{\leq}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|\leq D/2}\frac{(1-\sigma^{2})^{|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}}{p^{d|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|}}p^{d|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|/2}(d|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|)^{d|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|/2}\\
&\leq\sum_{\widebar{D}=1}^{D/2}\sum_{a=1}^{\widebar{D}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=\widebar{D},|I_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|=a}\frac{(1-\sigma^{2})^{\widebar{D}}}{p^{d\widebar{D}}}p^{d\widebar{D}/2}(d\widebar{D})^{d\widebar{D}/2}\\
&\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{\widebar{D}=1}^{D/2}\sum_{a=1}^{\widebar{D}}{n\choose a}{\widebar{D}-1\choose a-1}\frac{(1-\sigma^{2})^{\widebar{D}}}{p^{d\widebar{D}/2}}(d\widebar{D})^{d\widebar{D}/2}\end{split}

(61)

here (a) is because the set
\{\boldsymbol{\alpha}:|I_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|=a,|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|=\widebar{D}\}
has cardinality at most
{n\choose a}{\widebar{D}-1\choose a-1}
.

Step 2
. In this step, we bound
\sum_{a=1}^{\widebar{D}}{n\choose a}{\widebar{D}-1\choose a-1}
and complete the proof.

\begin{split}&\sum_{a=1}^{\widebar{D}}{n\choose a}{\widebar{D}-1\choose a-1}\\
\leq&\sum_{a=1}^{\widebar{D}}n^{a}{\widebar{D}-1\choose a-1}\leq n^{\widebar{D}}\sum_{a=1}^{\widebar{D}}{\widebar{D}-1\choose a-1}\leq(2n)^{\widebar{D}}.\end{split}

(62)

By plugging (
62
) into (
61
), we have

\begin{split}\sum_{(\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}):|\boldsymbol{\alpha}|+|\boldsymbol{\beta}|\leq D}\left(\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}h_{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})\right)^{2}&\leq\sum_{\widebar{D}=1}^{D/2}\left(2n(1-\sigma^{2})(d\widebar{D}/p)^{d/2}\right)^{\widebar{D}}\\
&\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{\widebar{D}=1}^{D/2}\delta^{\widebar{D}}\leq\frac{\delta}{1-\delta},\end{split}

here (a) is due to
n\leq\frac{\delta}{2(1-\sigma^{2})}(p/dD)^{d/2}
by our assumption. The proof is finished by observing (
57
).
\blacksquare

G.4
Hardness of Hypothesis Testing Implies Hardness of Estimation

Proposition 5
(Hardness of Hypothesis Testing Implies Hardness of Estimation)

If there does not exist a polynomial-time tester distinguishing between
H_{0}
and
H_{1}
in scalar-on-tensor regression with Type I + II error tending to zero as
n\to\infty
, then there is no polynomial-time estimator
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}
such that
\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}
as
n\to\infty
.

We prove this by using the sample splitting and contradiction argument. Suppose
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
and
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{2n}
are generated under
H_{1}
described in (
14
).

If there is polynomial-time estimator
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}
based on
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
such that
\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}
, then

\frac{3}{4}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}-\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}\leq\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}\leq\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{5}{4}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}.

Given the first half of the data, we consider performing the test based on the statistic

T=\sum_{i=n+1}^{2n}({\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{2}-1)\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle^{2}.

Under
H_{0}
,
\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}(T|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})=0
and

\begin{split}{\textrm{V}ar}_{H_{0}}(T|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})=n{\textrm{V}ar}_{H_{0}}(({\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{2}-1)\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle^{2})&=n\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}(({\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{2}-1)^{2})\mathbb{E}_{H_{0}}(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle^{2})\\
&=2n\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\frac{25}{8}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{\rm F}.\end{split}

Under
H_{1}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}(T|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})\\
=&n\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}\left(\left(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\rangle^{2}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}^{2}+2\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\rangle-1\right)\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle^{2}\right)\\
=&n\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}\left(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\rangle^{2}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle^{2}\right)\\
=&n\mathbb{E}_{H_{1}}\Bigg\{\left(\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in[p]^{\otimes d}}{\mathbfcal{A}}^{2}_{i,{\mathbf{j}}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*2}_{{\mathbf{j}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{j}}\in[p]^{\otimes d};{\mathbf{z}}\in[p]^{\otimes d}\\
{\mathbf{j}}\neq{\mathbf{z}}\end{subarray}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i,{\mathbf{j}}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i,{\mathbf{z}}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{j}}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{z}}}\right)\\
&\cdot\left(\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in[p]^{\otimes d}}{\mathbfcal{A}}^{2}_{i,{\mathbf{j}}}\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{2}_{{\mathbf{j}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{j}}\in[p]^{\otimes d};{\mathbf{z}}\in[p]^{\otimes d}\\
{\mathbf{j}}\neq{\mathbf{z}}\end{subarray}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i,{\mathbf{j}}}{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i,{\mathbf{z}}}\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{j}}}\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{z}}}\right)\Bigg\}\\
=&n\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{j}}\in[p]^{\otimes d};{\mathbf{z}}\in[p]^{\otimes d}\\
{\mathbf{j}}\neq{\mathbf{z}}\end{subarray}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*2}_{{\mathbf{j}}}\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{2}_{{\mathbf{z}}}+3\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in[p]^{\otimes d}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*2}_{{\mathbf{j}}}\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}^{2}_{{\mathbf{j}}}+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{j}}\in[p]^{\otimes d};{\mathbf{z}}\in[p]^{\otimes d}\\
{\mathbf{j}}\neq{\mathbf{z}}\end{subarray}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{j}}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}_{{\mathbf{z}}}\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{j}}}\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}_{{\mathbf{z}}}\right)\\
=&n(\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle^{2})\\
\overset{(a)}{\geq}&\frac{27}{16}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4},\end{split}

here (a) is because
\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{9}{16}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}
and

\begin{split}\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle=&\frac{1}{4}(\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}+\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\|_{\rm F}^{2})\\
\geq&\frac{1}{4}\left((\|2{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}-\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F})^{2}-\frac{1}{16}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\\
\geq&\frac{1}{4}\left((7/4)^{2}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}-1/16\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)=\frac{3}{4}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

Moreover,
{\textrm{V}ar}_{H_{1}}(T|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})=n{\textrm{V}ar}_{H_{1}}(({\mathbf{y}}_{i}^{2}-1)\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}\rangle^{2})=O(n)
.

So condition on
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, we consider the test of rejecting if
T\geq\frac{27}{32}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4}
, not rejecting otherwise. Then

\begin{split}\text{Type I error}&=\mathbb{P}_{H_{0}}(T\geq\frac{27}{32}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4}|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{{\textrm{V}ar}_{H_{0}}(T|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})}{(\frac{27}{32}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4})^{2}}\overset{n\to\infty}{\to}0,\\
\text{Type II error}&=\mathbb{P}_{H_{1}}(T\leq\frac{27}{32}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4}|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})=\mathbb{P}_{H_{1}}(T-\frac{27}{16}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4}\leq-\frac{27}{32}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4}|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})\\
&\leq\frac{{\textrm{V}ar}_{H_{1}}(T|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}})}{(\frac{27}{32}n\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{4})^{2}}\overset{n\to\infty}{\to}0,\end{split}

where (a) is by the Chebysev’s inequality.

So this polynomial-time test based on
T
achieves distinguishing between
H_{0}
and
H_{1}
with Type I + II errors goes to
0
as
n\to\infty
. This contradicts our assumption. So there does not exist a polynomial-time estimator
\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}
such that
\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{X}}}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}
and this finishes the proof.
\blacksquare

H
Proofs in Section
6

H.1
Proof of Lemma
3

Suppose
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}
has Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}
decomposition
\llbracket{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t},\ldots,{\mathbf{U}}_{d+m}^{t}\rrbracket
and
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
has Tucker rank
\mathbf{r}^{*}
decomposition
\llbracket{\mathbfcal{S}};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{U}}_{d+m}\rrbracket
, respectively. Recall

\begin{split}{\mathbf{W}}_{k}:=({\mathbf{U}}_{d+m}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k-1}\otimes\ldots\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{1}){\mathbf{V}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{-k},r^{*}_{k}};\\
{\mathbf{W}}^{t}_{k}:=({\mathbf{U}}^{t}_{d+m}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\mathbf{U}}^{t}_{k+1}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}^{t}_{k-1}\otimes\ldots\otimes{\mathbf{U}}^{t}_{1}){\mathbf{V}}^{t}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{-k},r_{k}},\end{split}

where
p_{-k}=\prod_{i\neq k}p_{i}
,
{\mathbf{V}}_{k}={\rm QR}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}})^{\top}),{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}={\rm QR}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t})^{\top})
. For
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}
, it can be decomposed in the following way

\begin{split}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}=&{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}}+{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t}}\times_{2}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2\perp}^{t}}+\cdots+{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{l=1}^{k-1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}\times_{k}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}}+\cdots+{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{l=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}\\
=&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{l=1}^{k-1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}\times_{k}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}}+{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{l=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}.\end{split}

(63)

For
k=1,\ldots,d
, let us denote
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}=[\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}\quad\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}]
where
\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}
is composed of the first
r_{k}^{*}
columns of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}
and
\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}
is composed of the remaining
(r_{k}-r_{k}^{*})
columns of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}
.

Then

\begin{split}P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}=&{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: tangent space projector}}{=}&{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}}+\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\mathcal{T}_{k}(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}}))\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: decom of Xstar}}{=}&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\left({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\times_{l=1}^{k-1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}\times_{k}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}}-\mathcal{T}_{k}(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})\right)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: matricization relationship}}{=}&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\left(\mathcal{T}_{k}\left(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})(\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})\right)\right)\\
=&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\left(\mathcal{T}_{k}\left(({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}})\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})(\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})\right)\right)\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: subspace-decomposition}}{=}&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\left(\mathcal{T}_{k}\left(({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})(\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})\right)\right)\\
\overset{(a)}{=}&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\left(\mathcal{T}_{k}\left(({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})(\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})\right)\right)\\
\overset{(b)}{=}&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\left(\mathcal{T}_{k}\left(({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{k}}-P_{\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})(\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})\right)\right),\end{split}

(64)

here (a) is because the
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
spans the column space of
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})
, (b) is because
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})(\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})=0
.

It is easy to check
\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}}
is a projection matrix. So from (
64
), we have

\begin{split}\|P_{(T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\leq&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\|\mathcal{T}_{k}\left((P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})(\otimes_{l=d+m}^{k+1}{\mathbf{I}}_{p_{l}}\otimes_{l=k-1}^{1}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}})\right)\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\leq&\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\|({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})\times_{k}(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}})\|_{{\rm F}}\\
\leq&(d+m)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}\|_{{\rm F}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,d+m}\|P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}}-P_{\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{t}}\|\\
\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: user-friendly-subspace-perturb}}{\leq}&2(d+m)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,d+m}\frac{\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})-\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})\|}{\sigma_{r^{*}_{k}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}))}\\
\leq&2(d+m)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|_{{\rm F}}\max_{k=1,\ldots,d+m}\frac{\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*})-\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t})\|_{\rm F}}{\sigma_{r^{*}_{k}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}))}\\
\leq&\frac{2(d+m)\|{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}-{\mathbfcal{X}}^{*}\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}}{\underline{\lambda}}.\end{split}

In the special matrix setting, i.e.,
d+m=2
, we can get a sharper bound.

\begin{split}&~~P_{(T_{{\mathbf{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}={\mathbf{X}}^{*}-P_{T_{{\mathbf{X}}^{t}}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\\
&=P_{{\mathbf{U}}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}+{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}}+P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}}\\
&=(P_{{\mathbf{U}}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}}){\mathbf{X}}^{*}+{\mathbf{X}}^{*}(P_{{\mathbf{V}}}-P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}})-P_{{\mathbf{U}}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}}+P_{{\mathbf{U}}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}}+P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}}\\
&=(P_{{\mathbf{U}}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}}){\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\mathbf{I}}-P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}})+({\mathbf{I}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}}){\mathbf{X}}^{*}(P_{{\mathbf{V}}}-P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}})\\
&\overset{(a)}{=}(P_{{\mathbf{U}}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}}){\mathbf{X}}^{*}({\mathbf{I}}-P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}})\\
&\overset{(b)}{=}(P_{{\mathbf{U}}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}})({\mathbf{X}}^{*}-{\mathbf{X}}^{t})({\mathbf{I}}-P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}}),\end{split}

(65)

where
{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r^{*}},{\mathbf{V}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{2},r^{*}}
are left and right singular vectors of
{\mathbf{X}}^{*}
, (a) is due to the fact that
({\mathbf{I}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}}){\mathbf{X}}^{*}(P_{{\mathbf{V}}}-P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}})=0
and (b) is because
{\mathbf{X}}^{t}({\mathbf{I}}-P_{{\mathbf{V}}^{t}})=0
. Thus

\begin{split}\|P_{(T_{{\mathbf{X}}^{t}})_{\perp}}{\mathbf{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\overset{\eqref{eq: decomposition of X-bar complement}}{\leq}\|P_{{\mathbf{U}}}-P_{{\mathbf{U}}^{t}}\|\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-{\mathbf{X}}^{t}\|_{\rm F}\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: user-friendly-subspace-perturb}}{\leq}\frac{2\|{\mathbf{X}}^{*}-{\mathbf{X}}^{t}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{\sigma_{r^{*}}({\mathbf{X}}^{*})}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof of this lemma.

\blacksquare

H.2
Proof of Theorem
10

Recall

\begin{split}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}&={\rm{SVD}}_{r_{i}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{i}(\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}\times_{j<i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top}\times_{j>i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top})\right)\\
&={\rm{SVD}}_{r_{i}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{T}}\times_{j<i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top}\times_{j>i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top})+\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{j<i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top}\times_{j>i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top})\right).\end{split}

Suppose
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}=[\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}\quad\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}]
where
\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}
is composed of the first
r_{i}^{*}
columns of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}
and
\widecheck{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}
contains the remaining
(r_{i}-r_{i}^{*})
columns of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}^{1}
.
First,

\begin{split}2\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}&\geq 2\|\left(\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{j<i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top}\times_{j>i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top})\right)_{\max(r_{i})}\|_{\rm F}\\
&\geq 2\|\left(\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{j<i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top}\times_{j>i}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})^{\top})\right)_{\max(r^{*}_{i})}\|_{\rm F}\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm:SVD-projection}}{\geq}\|\widebar{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i\perp}^{1\top}\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{T}})\otimes_{j\neq i}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0}\|_{\rm F}\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: subspace-decomposition}}{\geq}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i\perp}^{1\top}\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{T}})\otimes_{j\neq i}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}(\otimes_{j\neq i}{\mathbf{U}}_{j})^{\top}\otimes_{j\neq i}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0}\|_{\rm F}\\
&\geq\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i\perp}^{1\top}\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{T}})\|_{\rm F}\prod_{j\neq i}\sigma_{\min}({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})\\
&\overset{(a)}{\geq}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i\perp}^{1\top}\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{T}})\|_{\rm F}(1/2)^{(d-1)/2}\\
\Longrightarrow&\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i\perp}^{1\top}\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{T}})\|_{\rm F}\leq 2^{(d+1)/2}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}.\end{split}

(66)

where (a) is because

\begin{split}\sigma_{\min}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0})=\min_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{*}_{j}}}\frac{\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}^{2}}&=\min_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{*}_{j}}}\frac{\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}({\mathbf{I}}-\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j\perp}^{0}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j\perp}^{0\top})\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}^{2}}\\
&=\min_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{*}_{j}}}\frac{\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}-\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j\perp}^{0}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j\perp}^{0\top}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}^{2}}\\
&=\frac{\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}^{2}}-\max_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{*}_{j}}}\frac{\|{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j\perp}^{0}\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{a}}\|_{2}^{2}}\\
&=1-\|{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{j\perp}^{0}\|^{2}\geq 1/2,\end{split}

(67)

Finally, by Lemma
14
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widehat{{\mathbfcal{T}}}-{\mathbfcal{T}}\|_{\rm F}&=\|\widetilde{{\mathbfcal{T}}}\times_{i=1}^{d}P_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{1}_{i}}-{\mathbfcal{T}}\|_{{\rm F}}\\
&\leq\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\times_{i=1}^{d}P_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{1}_{i}}\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}^{1\top}_{i\perp}\mathcal{M}_{i}({\mathbfcal{T}})\|_{\rm F}\overset{\eqref{ineq: orthogonal-projection-error}}{\leq}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}+d\cdot 2^{(d+1)/2}\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{\max(\mathbf{r})}\|_{\rm F}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof.
\blacksquare

H.3
Proof of Lemma
4
.

We divide the proof into two steps: in step 1 we consider the setting
\alpha\leq\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}
and in step 2, we consider
\alpha>\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{w}
.

Step 1 (
\alpha\leq\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}
)
. Given
(Y,{\mathbf{X}})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}1&{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{u}}&{\mathbf{I}}_{w}\end{bmatrix}\right)
, let
Z=(Y-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w})/\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}
. First, by the construction and the property of multivariate Gaussian random vectors, we have
Z
follows Gaussian distribution with
\mathbb{E}[Z]=0
,
{\textrm{V}ar}(Z)=1
. Moreover
\text{cov}(Z,X_{j})=0
, so
Z
is independent of
X_{j}
for all
j=1,\ldots,w
by the property of Gaussian random variables. Given
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{w}
, let us define
\boldsymbol{\beta}!=\prod_{i=1}^{w}\beta_{i}!
. Then

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}(Y)\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(X_{j})\right)=&\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w}+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}Z\right)\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(X_{j})\right)\\
=&\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w}+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}Z\right)\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(X_{j})\Big|Z\right)\right)\\
\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: hermite-poly-integration-by-part}}{=}&\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\beta}!\alpha!}}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\partial\sqrt{\alpha!}h_{\alpha}(\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w}+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}Z)}{\partial X_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots\partial X_{w}^{\beta_{w}}}\Big|Z\right)\right)\\
\overset{(a)}{=}&\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\boldsymbol{\beta}!\alpha!}}\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\partial(\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w}+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}Z)^{\alpha}}{\partial X_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots\partial X_{w}^{\beta_{w}}}\Big|Z\right)\right)\\
\overset{(b)}{=}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha!}{\prod_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}!}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{\beta_{j}},&\text{if }\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{w}\beta_{j}\\
0,&\text{if }\alpha<\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}\end{array}\right..\end{split}

Here (a) is because for
\sqrt{\alpha!}h_{\alpha}(x)
is an order-
\alpha
polynomial and the coeffient with
x^{\alpha}
is 1 and the fact that except the polynomial
(\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w}+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}Z)^{\alpha}
, other terms in
\sqrt{\alpha!}h_{\alpha}(\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w}+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}Z)
will be zero after taking the derivative as
\alpha\leq\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}
; (b) is because in the expansion of
(\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{w}X_{w}+\sqrt{1-\sum_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{2}}Z)^{\alpha}
, the polynomial with respect to
(X_{1},\ldots,X_{w})
has degree at most
\alpha
, the fact
\alpha\leq\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}
and the coefficient of polynomial
X_{1}^{\beta_{1}}\cdots X_{q}^{\beta_{q}}
when
\alpha=\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}
is
\frac{\alpha!\prod_{j=1}^{w}u_{j}^{\beta_{j}}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}!}
.

Step 2 (
\alpha>\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{w}
)
. Given
(Y,{\mathbf{X}})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}1&{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}\\
{\mathbf{u}}&{\mathbf{I}}_{w}\end{bmatrix}\right)
, let
Z_{j}=(X_{j}-u_{j}Y)/\sqrt{1-u_{j}^{2}}
for
j=1,\ldots,w
. Since
(Y,{\mathbf{X}})
is multivariate normal distributed,
Z_{j}
s are also normal distributed. Moreover,
\mathbb{E}(Z_{j})=0
and
{\textrm{V}ar}(Z_{j})=1
. Finally,
\{Z_{j}\}_{j=1}^{w}
are independent of
Y
as
\text{cov}(Z_{j},Y)=0
for
j=1,\ldots,w
. Then

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}(Y)\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(X_{j})\right)&=\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}(Y)\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(u_{j}Y+\sqrt{1-u_{j}^{2}}Z_{j})\right)\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(h_{\alpha}(Y)\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(u_{j}Y+\sqrt{1-u_{j}^{2}}Z_{j})\Big|\{Z_{j}\}_{j=1}^{w}\right)\right)\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: hermite-poly-integration-by-part}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\partial\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(u_{j}Y+\sqrt{1-u_{j}^{2}}Z_{j})}{\partial Y^{\alpha}}\Big|\{Z_{j}\}_{j=1}^{w}\right)\right)\\
&\overset{(a)}{=}0,\end{split}

where (a) is because
\prod_{j=1}^{w}h_{\beta_{j}}(u_{j}Y+\sqrt{1-u_{j}^{2}}Z_{j})
is a polynomial of degree at most
\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}
in
Y
and
\alpha>\sum_{j=1}^{w}\beta_{j}
. This finishes the proof of this lemma.
\blacksquare

I
Proofs in Section
7

I.1
Properties of Contracted Tensor Inner Product

We first introduce the following lemma
12
, which reveals a few useful properties of the contracted tensor inner product defined in (
23
) and will be used frequently in deriving efficient implementation of RGN.

Lemma 12
(Properties of Contracted Tensor Inner Product)

Let
{\mathbfcal{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}\times p_{d+1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}}
,
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
,
{\mathbfcal{W}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{k-1}\times q_{k}\times p_{k+1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
be tensors with
d\geq k\geq 1
,
m\geq 0
. For any
{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{k}\times p_{k}}
, we have

\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\rangle_{*}=\langle{\mathbfcal{X}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\rangle_{*}.

(68)

For any
{\mathbf{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{d+j}\times p_{d+j}}
with
1\leq j\leq m
, we have

\langle{\mathbfcal{X}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle_{*}\times_{j}{\mathbf{B}}=\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{d+j}{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle_{*}.

(69)

Proof of Lemma
12
.
We divide the proof into two steps. In step 1, we prove (
68
) and in step 2, we prove (
69
). Throughout the proof, let
{\mathbf{e}}_{j}
be the standard
j
th base vector where
j
th entry is
1
and others are zero.

Step 1
. For any
i_{1}\in[p_{d+1}],\ldots,i_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]
,

\begin{split}\langle{\mathbfcal{X}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\rangle_{*[i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}]}\overset{\eqref{def: contracted-tensor-inner-product}}{=}&\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}_{[:,\ldots,:,i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}]},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\rangle\\
=&\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l-d}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\rangle\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: matricization relationship}}{=}&\langle{\mathbf{A}}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l-d}}),\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{W}})\rangle\\
=&\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}}\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l-d}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\rangle\\
=&\langle({\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}})_{[:,\ldots,:,i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}]},{\mathbfcal{W}}\rangle\\
\overset{\eqref{def: contracted-tensor-inner-product}}{=}&\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbfcal{W}}\rangle_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}]}.\end{split}

Step 2
. For any
i_{1}\in[p_{d+1}],\ldots,i_{j-1}\in[p_{d+j-1}],i_{j}\in[q_{d+j}],i_{j+1}\in[p_{d+j+1}],\ldots,i_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]
,

\begin{split}&\left(\langle{\mathbfcal{X}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle_{*}\times_{j}{\mathbf{B}}\right)_{[i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}]}\\
=&\langle{\mathbfcal{X}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle_{*}\times_{j}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{j}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}\times_{l\neq j}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l}}^{\top}\\
=&\sum_{z_{1}\in[p_{d+1}],\ldots,z_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]}({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{j}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}})_{[z_{j}]}\left(\prod_{l\neq j}({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l}})_{[z_{l}]}\right)\langle{\mathbfcal{X}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle_{*[z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]}\\
\overset{\eqref{def: contracted-tensor-inner-product}}{=}&\sum_{z_{1}\in[p_{d+1}],\ldots,z_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]}\left(({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{j}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}})_{[z_{j}]}\prod_{l\neq j}({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l}})_{[z_{l}]}\right)\left(\sum_{w_{1}\in[p_{1}],\ldots,w_{d}\in[p_{d}]}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{[w_{1},\ldots,w_{d},z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]}{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{[w_{1},\ldots,w_{d}]}\right)\\
=&\sum_{w_{1}\in[p_{1}],\ldots,w_{d}\in[p_{d}]}{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{[w_{1},\ldots,w_{d}]}\left(\sum_{z_{1}\in[p_{d+1}],\ldots,z_{m}\in[p_{d+m}]}{\mathbfcal{X}}_{[w_{1},\ldots,w_{d},z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}]}({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{j}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}})_{[z_{j}]}\prod_{l\neq j}({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l}})_{[z_{l}]}\right)\\
=&\sum_{w_{1}\in[p_{1}],\ldots,w_{d}\in[p_{d}]}{\mathbfcal{Z}}_{[w_{1},\ldots,w_{d}]}\cdot\left({\mathbfcal{X}}_{[w_{1},\ldots,w_{d},:,\ldots,:]}\times_{d+j}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{j}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}\times_{l=d+1,l\neq d+j}^{d+m}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l-d}}\right)\\
=&\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},({\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{d+j}{\mathbf{B}})\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{e}}_{i_{l-d}}\rangle=\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},({\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{d+j}{\mathbf{B}})_{[:,\ldots,:,i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}]}\rangle\\
\overset{\eqref{def: contracted-tensor-inner-product}}{=}&\langle{\mathbfcal{X}}\times_{d+j}{\mathbf{B}},{\mathbfcal{Z}}\rangle_{*[:,\ldots,:,i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}]}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof of this lemma.
\blacksquare

I.2
Proof of Lemma
5

First, (
19
) follows from (
18
) by the relationship between the spectrum of a linear operator and its inverse, so we just need to show (
18
).

The claim (
18
) is equivalent to say the spectrum of
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}
is lower and upper bounded by
1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}
and
1+R_{2\mathbf{r}}
, respectively, for
{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
. Since
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}
is a symmetric operator, its spectrum can be upper bounded by
\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}=1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\rangle
and lower bounded by
\inf_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}=1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\rangle
. Also

\begin{split}&\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}=1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\rangle=\sup_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}=1}\|\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}\overset{(a)}{\leq}1+R_{2\mathbf{r}}\\
&\inf_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}=1}\langle{\mathbfcal{Z}},P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\rangle=\inf_{{\mathbfcal{Z}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}:\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}=1}\|\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|^{2}_{{\rm F}}\overset{(a)}{\geq}1-R_{2\mathbf{r}}.\end{split}

Here (a) is by the TRIP condition for
\mathscr{A}
,
P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})
is at most Tucker rank
2\mathbf{r}
by Lemma
7
and the fact
\|P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Z}})\|_{\rm F}\leq\|{\mathbfcal{Z}}\|_{{\rm F}}
.

\blacksquare

I.3
Proof of Proposition
2

First, for convenience of presentation, given
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
, we define
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(1)}={\mathbf{U}}_{k},{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(2)}={\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}
. By orthogonality, we know for any tensor
{\mathbfcal{T}}
of dimension
p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d+m}
,

\begin{split}\|{\mathbfcal{T}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=&\|{\mathbfcal{T}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}(P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}}+P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\left\|\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+m}=1}^{2}{\mathbfcal{T}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(s_{k})}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
=&\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+m}=1}^{2}\left\|{\mathbfcal{T}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}P_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(s_{k})}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+m}=1}^{2}\left\|{\mathbfcal{T}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(s_{k})\top}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(70)

By the parameterization form of the tangent space of
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}
given in (
24
), we know to solve the RGN update, i.e.,
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}=\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{{\mathbfcal{X}}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}}\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}-\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{X}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}
, it is equivalent to solve

\begin{split}&({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\}_{k=1}^{d+m})\\
=&\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbfcal{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d+m}},\\
{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{(p_{k}-r_{k})\times r_{k}},k=1,\ldots,d+m\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}\rangle_{*}-\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\rangle_{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(71)

Next, let us decompose each term in the summand on the right-hand side of (
71
).

\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}\rangle_{*}-\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\rangle_{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: tensor-norm-decomp}}{=}&\sum_{s_{d+1},\ldots,s_{d+m}=1}^{2}\Big\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t(s_{l+d})\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}\rangle_{*}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t(s_{l+d})\top}\\
&\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\rangle_{*}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t(s_{l+d})\top}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: contracted-tensor-inner-product}}{=}&\sum_{s_{d+1},\ldots,s_{d+m}=1}^{2}\Big\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t(s_{l+d})\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},({\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t})\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t(s_{l})\top}\rangle_{*}\\
&\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t})\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t(s_{l})\top}\rangle_{*}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&=\sum_{s_{d+1},\ldots,s_{d+m}=1}^{2}\Big\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t(s_{l+d})\top}-\underbrace{\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t(s_{l})\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}\rangle_{*}}_{\text{(I)}}\\
&\quad-\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j=1,j\neq k}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\times_{l=d+1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t(s_{l})\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}\rangle_{*}}_{\text{(II)}}\\
&\quad-\underbrace{\sum_{k=d+1}^{d+m}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t(s_{k})\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{l=d+1,l\neq k}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t(s_{l})\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{l}^{t}\rangle_{*}}_{\text{(III)}}\Big\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(72)

Notice that (I) and (II) are non-zero only if
s_{l}=1
for all
l=d+1,\ldots,d+m
; (III) is non-zero only when there is one
z\in\{d+1,\ldots,d+m\}
such that
s_{z}=2
and for all other
l\neq z
,
s_{l}=1
; moreover, suppose
s_{z}=2
for some
z\in\{d+1,\ldots,d+m\}
, then

\text{(III)}=\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\times_{z}{\mathbf{D}}_{z}\rangle_{*}.

With the above observation, (
72
) can be simplified as

\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}\rangle_{*}-\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\rangle_{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
=&\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{B}}\times_{k=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}\rangle_{*}-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\times_{j=1,j\neq k}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\rangle_{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&+\sum_{z=1}^{m}\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{z}{\mathbf{U}}_{z+d\perp}^{t\top}\times_{l\neq z}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}\times_{z+d}{\mathbf{D}}_{z+d}\rangle_{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: contracted-tensor-inner-product}}{=}&\underbrace{\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{k=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{B}}\rangle_{*}-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\times_{j=1,j\neq k}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\rangle_{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}_{(T_{0})}\\
&+\sum_{z=1}^{m}\underbrace{\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{z}{\mathbf{U}}_{z+d\perp}^{t\top}\times_{l\neq z}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{z+d}{\mathbf{D}}_{z+d}\rangle_{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}_{(T_{z})}\end{split}

We note that
(T_{0})
only involves
({\mathbfcal{B}},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{d})
and
(T_{z})
only involves
{\mathbf{D}}_{z+d}
for
z=1,\ldots,m
. Moreover, we can view
(T_{0})
and
\{(T_{z})\}_{z=1}^{m}
as
(m+1)
separate least squares for the reasons below. First,

\begin{split}(T_{0})&=\sum_{j_{l}\in[r_{d+l}],l=1,\ldots,m}\Big(\left({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}\right)_{[j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{B}}\rangle_{*[j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}-\\
&\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\times_{j=1,j\neq k}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\rangle_{*[j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}\Big)^{2}\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: contracted-tensor-inner-product},\eqref{eq: matricization relationship}}{=}\sum_{j_{l}\in[r_{d+l}],l=1,\ldots,m}\Big(\left({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l=1}^{m}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}\right)_{[j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{B}}_{[:,\ldots,:,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]}\rangle-\\
&\quad-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\langle{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}\left({\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top}\right)\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}_{[:,\ldots,:,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m}]})\right)^{\top},{\mathbf{D}}_{k}\rangle\Big)^{2}.\end{split}

Moreover,

\begin{split}(T_{z})\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref {lm: contracted-tensor-inner-product}}{=}&\left\|{\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{z}{\mathbf{U}}_{z+d\perp}^{t\top}\times_{l\neq z}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top}-\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\rangle_{*}\times_{z}{\mathbf{D}}_{z+d}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: matricization relationship}}{=}&\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{z+d\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{z}({\mathbfcal{Y}}_{i}\times_{l\neq z}{\mathbf{U}}_{l+d}^{t\top})-{\mathbf{D}}_{z+d}\mathcal{M}_{z}(\langle{\mathbfcal{A}}_{i}\times_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top},{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\rangle_{*})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},\end{split}

notice that the above formula can be viewed as a multivariate least squares. Finally, the proposition follows by considering the sum of
(T_{0})
,
\{(T_{z})\}_{z=1}^{m}
over sample indices
i
in (
71
).
\blacksquare

I.4
Proof of Lemma
6

By computing the gradient of the objective in (
8
) and setting it to zero,
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}
can be uniquely solved by the following linear system:

P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\mathscr{A}^{*}\mathscr{A}P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5})=P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}(\mathscr{A}^{*}({\mathbfcal{Y}})).

(73)

In this setting, it reduces to

P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}\left({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}\right)=P_{T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}).

(74)

Since
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\in T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
, we known from (
24
) that
{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}
can be written as

{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}={\mathbfcal{B}}^{t}\times_{k=1}^{1+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t}+\sum_{k=1}^{1+m}{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t},

(75)

for some to be determined
({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\}_{k=1}^{1+m})
.

•

Let us first compute
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t})
for
k=2,\ldots,1+m
. By the projection operation onto the tangent space
T_{{\mathbfcal{X}}^{t}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathbf{r}}
given in (
5
), we have for
k=2,\ldots,1+m
,

\begin{split}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}\left({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}\right){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}={\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}.\end{split}

(76)

By plugging (
75
) into (
76
), we get

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}\left({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}\right){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}\\
=&{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\times_{i\neq k,i\neq 1}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}\\
=&{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t})\left(\otimes_{i\neq 1,i\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top}\otimes({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}})\right){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}\\
=&{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t})\left(\otimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq k,\\
i\neq 1\end{subarray}}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{i}}\otimes({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t})\right){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}\\
\overset{(a)}{=}&{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t\top}\left(\otimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq k,\\
i\neq 1\end{subarray}}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{i}}\otimes({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t})\right){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}\\
=&{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}.\end{split}

(77)

Here (a) is because
{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}
by definition spans the row space of
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t})
. The last equality in (
77
) implies

{\mathbf{D}}_{k}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}={\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}\left({\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t\top}\left(\otimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq k,\\
i\neq 1\end{subarray}}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{i}}\otimes({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t})\right){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}\right)^{-1}.

(78)

So

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{k}\times_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t})\\
=&{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{\top}\otimes_{j\neq k}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq: DktMkS}}{=}&{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t}\left({\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t\top}\left(\otimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq k,\\
i\neq 1\end{subarray}}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{i}}\otimes({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t})\right){\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{t}\right)^{-1}{\mathbf{W}}_{k}^{t\top}.\end{split}

•

Now we compute
\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t})
and
\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{1}\times_{j\neq 1}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t})
. Similar to (
76
), here by projecting both sides of (
74
) onto the core and the first mode, we have

\begin{split}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}\left({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}\right){\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t}&={\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t},\\
\left({\mathbfcal{X}}^{t+0.5}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}\right)\times_{k=1}^{1+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t\top}&=({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\times_{k=1}^{1+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t\top}.\end{split}

(79)

By plugging (
75
) into (
79
), we get

{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}={\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t},

(80)

and

{\mathbfcal{B}}^{t}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t}+{\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{t}=({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\times_{k=1}^{1+m}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{t\top}.

(81)

Do matricization along mode-
1
on both sides of (
81
) yields

{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t})+{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t})={\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\otimes_{j=(1+m)}^{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t}.

(82)

By multiplying
{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}
on both sides of (
82
) and combining with (
80
) yields

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}+{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}=\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t}\\
\Longrightarrow&{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}=({\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t}\\
\overset{(a)}{\Longrightarrow}&{\mathbf{D}}_{1}^{t}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}={\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}({\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t},\end{split}

(83)

here (a) is by multiplying
{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}
on both sides of the equation. So

\begin{split}&\quad\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{1}\times_{j\neq 1}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t})\\
&={\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t})\otimes_{j=(1+m)}^{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top}\\
&={\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{D}}^{t}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{S}}^{t}){\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{t\top}\otimes_{j=(1+m)}^{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{j}^{t\top}\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq: mode-1-part2}}{=}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{t\top}({\mathbf{A}}^{\top}{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{Y}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}){\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t}{\mathbf{W}}_{1}^{t\top}.\end{split}

Finally by (
82
), we have

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathbfcal{B}}^{t})\\
